A TF-IDF and logistic regression baseline for binary sentiment classification of short texts

Mustafa Ali Mohsin, Salam Abdulkhaleq Noaman — 2026-11-08 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.55670/fpll.futech.5.4.13

The rapid growth of social media has created a large amount of short-text data that expresses people's feelings and opinions on many topics; therefore, sentiment classification has become a popular problem to address. This paper focuses on binary sentiment classification using the Sentiment 140 dataset of 1.6 million labeled tweets. A preprocessing pipeline, which includes text normalization, URL removal, and noise cleaning, was applied to the original dataset. Text representation was conducted using term frequency-inverse document frequency with an n-gram model. Logistic Regression algorithm was used for classification. The paper includes experimental evaluations using common metrics, such as accuracy, precision, recall, F1-score, ROC-AUC, the confusion matrix, and the precision-recall graph. Results show that the combination of TF-IDF feature extraction and a Logistic Regression classifier produced consistent results and achieved a computationally inexpensive baseline classifier with 85.2% accuracy, 0.852 F1-score, and 0.93 ROC-AUC when performing the Sentiment140 binary classification task. These results strongly suggest that traditional machine learning algorithms can still provide highly accurate models for such a large-scale classification challenge. Despite this, the current model has limitations in understanding sarcasm and linguistic idioms, suggesting an opportunity for deep learning and transformer-based models to outperform.

Autopsy findings after SARS-CoV-2 vaccination: a German national registry study

Saskia Freifrau von Stillfried, Roman David Bülow, Reinhard B Dettmeyer, Benjamin Ondruschka, Carolin Edler — 2026-11-07 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1007/s00428-026-04754-1

Abstract Autopsies are the foundation of evidence-based medicine and gold standard for determining causes of death. A potential causal relationship between COVID-19 vaccination and death remains a subject of debate, however multidisciplinary, multicentric national autopsy studies are missing. N = 486 eligible SARS-CoV-2 vaccinated autopsy cases from 14 German pathology, neuropathology and legal medicine university centres between 2021 and 2024 were available via the German National Autopsy Registry. For comparison COVID-19 autopsies were included ( n = 385 cases, all performed before the vaccination campaign in Germany started). A previously established causality algorithm was applied in a consensus assessment of potential causality between vaccine administration and an adverse event following immunization (AEFI). N = 176 cases with a post vaccination interval ≤ 28d were included into analyses. In a single case of allergic reaction, in 5/8 cases of either myocarditis ( n = 4 cases) or perimyocarditis ( n = 1 case), and 9/26 cases of thromboembolism, the consensus process resulted in a potential AEFI in these cases, but no definite causality. In n = 3 cases in which the cause of death remained unclear after autopsy, no potential AEFI could be established. In SARS-CoV-2 positive autopsy cases without vaccination, thromboembolism was documented as cause of death in 18/385 cases and myocarditis in 2/385 cases. In conclusion, autopsies are a valuable tool to assess adverse effects. Currently, post vaccination autopsy numbers are too low to draw conclusions on the general population or to identify rare AEFI signals. As part of pandemic preparedness, international guidelines for post vaccination autopsy procedures, diagnostics and interpretation are necessary. Autopsy registries can support this collective effort.

First contact: biophysical insights into non-enveloped viruses from VLPs to packed virions

Jennifer Rothe, Charlotte Uetrecht — 2026-11-07 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1016/j.coviro.2026.101589

Precise biophysical characterization of non-enveloped viruses and virus-like particles (VLPs) is essential for evaluating assembly, structural integrity, and sample heterogeneity — key parameters that influence infectivity, manufacturability, and therapeutic performance. Analytical ultracentrifugation, light scattering, and mass photometry provide complementary information on particle size distributions, empty/full ratios, and assembly quality. Native and charge-detection mass spectrometry extend this, enabling accurate mass determination and genome-loading assessment. Solution-based structural techniques, such as SAXS and spectroscopy, provide dynamics. High-resolution X-ray crystallography, cryogenic electron microscopy, and tomography reveal global architectures, quaternary arrangements, and conformational switching essential for entry and maturation. Besides these methods, atomic force microscopy also allows nanoscale visualization in conjunction with mechanical profiling, linking physical stability with functional behavior. Thermal, chemical, and mechanical stress assays offer insight into unfolding pathways, pH-dependent disassembly, and genome-driven capsid pressurization, informing both viral biology and formulation science. Finally, mass spectrometry-based methods enable site-specific mapping of post-translational modifications and conformational perturbations. Together, these approaches establish a comprehensive framework for characterizing viral particles and VLPs, supporting rational engineering of stable, homogeneous, and functional assemblies for vaccines, gene delivery, and nanobiotechnology. This review summarizes current label-free methods for assessing size, mass, structural organization, and stability of intact viral particles.

Organ-Selective Lipid Nanoparticles: A Landscape Review of Mechanisms, Applications, and Translational Challenges

Rumiana D. Koynova — 2026-11-07 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1021/acsptsci.6c00553

Abstract Lipid nanoparticles (LNPs) are the dominant clinical platform for nucleic acid delivery, as shown by liver-directed patisiran and intramuscular mRNA-based COVID-19 vaccines. Neither involved engineered organ selectivity. Conventional four-component LNPs accumulate mainly in the liver after systemic administration, which limits their use to hepatocyte-targeted therapies. Selective organ targeting (SORT) adds a fifth, charged “SORT lipid” to the formulation. This redirects payloads to the lung, spleen, or liver in a predictable, dose-titratable manner. The leading proposed explanation is that SORT lipid alters the protein corona that forms in blood, shifting uptake away from the apolipoprotein E–low-density lipoprotein receptor pathway behind default hepatic accumulation. Consensus on this mechanism is lacking. Lipid charge and pKa, route-dependent hemodynamics, and vascular fenestration may contribute independently or in combination. We review mechanistic and translational evidence for lung-, spleen-, and liver-selective SORT formulations across three tiers: rodent, higher-order preclinical, and clinical or clinical-adjacent. We also cover bone marrow, tumor, and CNS targets and payload-specific demands. A bibliometric analysis of administration routes, corona proteins, cell types, payloads, and diseases complements this synthesis. Research volume concentrates on intravenous delivery, oncology/immunotherapy, and cystic fibrosis. Bone marrow, broader immune cell, and LSEC targeting remains comparatively underexplored. Associations between corona composition and tropism are more robust than demonstrated causality, and most direct evidence still comes from rodents, not primates or humans. We outline translational gaps and propose machine learning-guided lipid screening, combinatorial SORT–ligand targeting, and patient-specific corona profiling as priority directions for extrahepatic gene therapy, in vivo cell engineering, and precision vaccination.

Albert Carcereny, David García-Pedemonte, Ian Pérez, Susana Guix, María Isabel Costafreda — 2026-11-07 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1002/imo2.70153

The final phases of the Global Polio Eradication Initiative are challenged by the circulation of vaccine-derived polioviruses, particularly serotype 2, occurring even in certified polio-free countries. Integrated environmental surveillance and long-read deep sequencing enable resolution of the viral haplotype network. Structural modelling of this network reveals the poliovirus phenotypic landscape. Self-Dissociating Albumin Nanoclusters for Passive Lymph Node-Targeted Inverse Vaccination in a Rheumatoid Arthritis Model

Minjae Kim, Yina Wu, Na-Young Yu, Pil-Jae Song, Gyulim Kim — 2026-11-07 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1021/acsnano.6c16289

Abstract Engineering lymph node-targeted tolerogenic nanomaterials requires simultaneous antigen–drug coloading and efficient lymphatic transport. However, conventional biocompatible nanoparticles with static architectures often face a size-dependent trade-off: larger particles enable cargo coloading but drain inefficiently to lymph nodes, whereas smaller particles favor lymphatic transport but have limited cargo versatility. Here, a size-transformable, self-dissociating albumin nanocluster inverse vaccine is introduced to address this materials design trade-off. Citrullinated peptide antigens are conjugated to human serum albumin, and rapamycin (RAPA) is coloaded using ball-milling technology to generate 200–300 nm nanoclusters. After subcutaneous injection, the nanoclusters dissociate into sub-10 nm albumin-based units, enabling passive lymphatic transport and accumulation in draining lymph nodes while maintaining coordinated antigen–RAPA codelivery. In vitro mechanistic studies show that the nanoclusters induce a tolerogenic dendritic cell phenotype and promote antigen-specific immune regulation. In a collagen-induced arthritis mouse model, weekly administration attenuates synovial inflammation, humoral autoimmune responses, bone erosion, and cartilage degradation while expanding Foxp3+ regulatory T cells. These findings establish self-dissociation-mediated size transformation as a functional materials strategy for lymph node-targeted antigen-specific immune tolerance and tolerogenic nanomedicine.

Primary Evaluation of Immune Responses in Mice After Immunization With Ferritin Nanoparticle‐Displayed Taenia solium TSOL18

Keke Wu, Liqun Wang, Xiao‐Yu Cao, Li‐Yang Wei, Ying‐Hui Xiu — 2026-11-07 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1096/fj.202603362rr

ABSTRACT Cysticercosis, caused by the larval stage of Taenia solium , remains a major neglected tropical disease with limited vaccine options. Although the oncospheral antigen TSOL18 confers efficient protection, its immunogenicity and durability remain suboptimal. Here, we engineered a ferritin‐based nanoparticle vaccine platform to improve TSOL18 antigen features, presentation efficiency and immune activation. The mature TSOL18 protein (lacking the N‐terminal signal peptide) was genetically fused to Helicobacter pylori ferritin either alone (TF) or together with a multiepitope peptide (GK1, KETc1, KETc12) and CD40 (TMCF), generating self‐assembling nanoparticles of approximately 15 nm in diameter, as confirmed by transmission electron microscopy, dynamic light scattering and immunogold labeling. All formulations exhibited endotoxin levels below the United States Pharmacopeia‐recommended limit (5 EU/kg), and safety assessment in mice revealed no significant alterations in serum alanine aminotransferase, aspartate aminotransferase, or ferritin levels, indicating a favorable safety profile. Compared with soluble TSOL18, the stability of nanoantigens was significantly improved, and both nanoparticle formulations (administered at equimolar doses with 20 μg soluble TSOL18) elicited significantly higher and more sustained TSOL18‐specific antibody responses than the non‐nanoparticle antigen, accompanied by enhanced CD4 + T cell activation and a Th2‐skewed cytokine profile characterized by elevated interleukin‐4 (IL‐4) production in BALB/c mice. Notably, incorporation of immunostimulatory epitopes further augmented cellular immune responses. Collectively, these findings demonstrate that ferritin‐mediated nanoparticle display substantially improves the magnitude and quality of TSOL18‐induced immunity while maintaining a favorable safety profile, providing a scalable strategy to overcome key limitations of current cysticercosis subunit vaccines.

Community-acquired pneumonia in Europe: heterogeneity, evidence gaps, and priorities for care

Ignacio Martín‐Loeches, Antoní Torres — 2026-11-07 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1016/j.lanepe.2026.101910

Community-acquired pneumonia (CAP) remains a leading cause of hospital and intensive care admission and infectious death in Europe, particularly among older adults, people with multimorbidity, and immunocompromised patients. This Review argues that persistent uncertainty in CAP evidence largely reflects heterogeneity across eight domains: case definition, host, pathogen ascertainment, severity, inflammatory response, treatment exposure, timing and trajectory, and outcome measurement. We examine how these domains distort incidence estimates, pathogen rankings, diagnostic accuracy, severity classification, and trial findings. Within this framework, we highlight developments relevant to Europe: randomised vaccine trials assessing hospitalisation rather than immunogenicity; national data suggesting a changing, increasingly β-lactam-resistant role for Haemophilus influenzae ; evidence suggesting that discordant corticosteroid trials reflect heterogeneity of treatment effect rather than drug or protocol differences; and the first randomised trial of host-response-directed immunotherapy in pneumonia-predominant sepsis. We also examine continuing fragmentation in European adult vaccination policy, surveillance, and diagnostic capacity. We propose a diagnostic approach organised by clinical group, a minimum CAP dataset with explicit governance, and a research agenda separating interventions applicable now, evidence required before implementation, and unresolved questions. This approach connects advances in prevention, diagnosis, and treatment with the methodological and organisational changes needed to generate more reliable, clinically actionable European evidence.

Network-based SEIVQHR modelling with optimal mobility control and QUBO reformulation

Sélectionné · envoyé par email

R. Ramesh, M. Kannan, K. Chandrasekran, Longjun Zhan — 2026-11-07 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1038/s41598-026-79134-5

Abstract Human mobility between interconnected geographical regions can substantially influence the spatial transmission of infectious diseases, motivating epidemic-control strategies that account for both disease dynamics and regional interactions. In this study, we develop a network-based discrete-time SEIVQHR model comprising susceptible, exposed, infectious, vaccinated, quarantined, hospitalized, and recovered compartments, with inter-regional transmission represented through a normalized mobility matrix. The formulation incorporates vaccination, quarantine, hospitalization, and mobility-dependent infection within a unified metapopulation framework. A key feature of the model is the explicit representation of breakthrough infection among vaccinated individuals. Vaccine efficacy $$\epsilon $$ determines the residual susceptibility $$1-\epsilon $$ , and breakthrough infections enter the exposed compartment before progressing through the infectious pathway. We establish positivity, boundedness, and positive invariance of the discrete-time system under admissibility conditions involving the time step $$\Delta t$$ . The basic reproduction number $$R_0$$ is derived using a next-generation matrix formulation that accounts for both vaccination coverage and network connectivity. The disease-free equilibrium is shown to be locally asymptotically stable for $$R_0<1$$ and unstable for $$R_0>1$$ . We further formulate a finite-horizon optimal-control problem with binary regional mobility restrictions and continuous vaccination allocation subject to vaccine-supply constraints. For a prescribed vaccination trajectory, the binary mobility-control component is approximated locally by a quadratic objective and reformulated as a Quadratic Unconstrained Binary Optimization (QUBO) problem. The resulting QUBO is mapped to an equivalent Ising Hamiltonian, enabling solution using simulated annealing, greedy heuristics, or quantum-annealing platforms. The framework is calibrated using COVID-19 surveillance data from India for 2021, with selected epidemiological parameters fixed at literature-based values to reduce overfitting and maintain biological plausibility. Numerical experiments examine parameter sensitivity, reproduction-number behaviour, admissible parameter regions, QUBO construction, and solver performance. The results identify the transmission rate as the dominant sensitivity factor and demonstrate the feasibility of the proposed QUBO-based formulation for binary mobility-control optimization. Overall, the proposed framework provides a mathematically consistent decision-support methodology that connects network-based epidemic modelling, continuous vaccination allocation, binary mobility intervention, and QUBO/Ising optimization while retaining the nonlinear epidemic dynamics for validation of the resulting intervention strategies.

Aerial Health: Foundations of an Emerging Multidisciplinary Healthcare Discipline.

Medhat Zohery — 2026-11-07 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.24043491

The development of unmanned aerial vehicles (UAVs), autonomous aerial platforms, artificial intelligence, advanced communication systems, and electric vertical take-off and landing (eVTOL) technologies is creating new possibilities for healthcare delivery. Existing applications include the transportation of medical supplies, blood products, vaccines, automated external defibrillators, biological samples, and diagnostic equipment, as well as emergency response, aerial surveillance, and remote healthcare support. However, these applications are frequently investigated as separate technological or operational solutions rather than as interconnected components of a unified healthcare system. This paper proposes Aerial Health as an emerging multidisciplinary healthcare discipline concerned with the design, development, deployment, coordination, integration, and evaluation of aerial technologies and systems for providing, supporting, and extending healthcare services. It establishes the conceptual foundations, scope, principles, theoretical dimensions, research domains, system architecture, and operational processes of Aerial Health. The paper introduces the AERIAL Conceptual Framework, comprising six interdependent dimensions: Assessment of Health Needs; Engineering of Aerial Platforms and Payloads; Routing, Autonomy and Resource Coordination; Integration with Healthcare Systems; Assurance, Safety and Governance; and Learning, Evaluation and Optimization. It further proposes an integrated Aerial Healthcare Systems Architecture connecting patients, healthcare professionals, medical institutions, aerial platforms, medical resources, communication networks, intelligent coordination systems, operational infrastructure, and regulatory authorities. The framework provides a foundation for the development of Aerial Health as a coherent research domain and establishes a research agenda covering aerial emergency healthcare, medical logistics, telemedicine, diagnostics, public health, autonomous healthcare systems, and heterogeneous aerial healthcare networks.

Immunogenicity and safety of two priming doses of an MF59-adjuvanted cell-based H5N8 influenza vaccine in healthy persons aged ≥18 years

Adam B. Brosz, Janine Oberije, Eve M. Versage, Arik Elfassy, Derchieh Hung — 2026-11-07 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1016/j.vaccine.2026.129279

Background Increased transmission of avian influenza from animals to humans has elevated the risk of an H5 pandemic. Methods This phase 2, randomized, observer-blind, multi-center trial assessed the immunogenicity and safety of homologous priming with an MF59-adjuvanted, cell-based H5N8 A/Astrakhan/3212/2020 clade 2.3.4.4b vaccine (aH5N8c). We enrolled adults age ≥18 years, equally stratified into 18–64 (including a poultry-worker subset) and ≥65 year cohorts. Primary and secondary outcomes were homologous antibody responses against H5N8 measured in all participants by hemagglutination inhibition (HI) and in a subset by microneutralization (MN) assays on Days 1, 22, and 43. Solicited adverse events (AEs) were monitored for 7 days post-vaccination, with unsolicited AEs tracked throughout Day 43. Results 239 participants were vaccinated (18–64 years, n = 121; ≥65 years, n = 118). At Day 43 (21 days post second aH5N8c dose), geometric mean fold increases (GMFI) were 13.7 for the 18–64 cohort and 10.2 for the ≥65 cohort. HI seroconversion rates were 75.8% (95% CI 66.1–83.8%) for the 18–64 cohort and 71.0% (61.1–79.6%) for the ≥65 cohort. HI titers ≥1:40 were achieved in 75.8% (66.1–83.8%) and 73.0% (63.2–81.4%) of participants, respectively. MN seroconversion rates were 80.6% (68.6–89.6%) and 70.0% (56.8–81.2%), while MN titers ≥1:40 were 83.9% (72.3–92.0%) and 86.7% (75.4–94.1%), respectively. No notable differences in immune responses by poultry worker status were observed. Most solicited AEs were mild or moderate, resolving within 3 days. Unsolicited AEs were reported by 16.7% participants by Day 43. Conclusion These data support the use of the MF59-adjuvanted, cell-based monovalent H5N8 vaccine as a public health intervention against H5 influenza.

Sclerosing Activity and Thrombogenic Potential of a COVID-19 mRNA Vaccine Metabolite: New Evidence of Its Contribution to the Pathogenesis of Adverse Cardiovascular Reactions, Including Fatal Outcomes

Gabrielle Segalla — 2026-11-07 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.56098/qt8y6390

A previous study published in this journal identified a fundamental inconsistency in the pharmacokinetic dossiers for Pfizer/BioNTech’s Comirnaty mRNA vaccine: the substitution, in the documentation, of the actual ALC-0315 metabolite, 2-hexyldecanoic acid, with the “phantom”, untraceable isomer 6-hexyldecanoic acid. Beyond circumventing the Aquatic Chronic 1 (H410) toxicological classification and the inhibition of mitochondrial β-oxidation attributable to steric hindrance from branching at C2, chemical, historical and pharmacological evidence now points to substantially more serious concerns. Authoritative chemical reference works explicitly classify 2-hexyldecanoic acid and its sodium salt as “sclerosing agents”. This review examines the membrane-disruptive and endothelium-damaging activity of the anionic form of 2-hexyldecanoic acid, a detergent surfactant, and analyses its direct relationship with the induction of tissue necrosis, chemical phlebitis and the ensuing thrombogenic cascade. These mechanisms provide an incontrovertible biochemical and aetiopathogenic rationale for the occurrence of severe cardiovascular adverse events - including thrombosis, myocarditis and pericarditis - observed after vaccination on a large scale, constituting an unprecedented violation of the Precautionary Principle.

Lipid Nanoparticles for RNA Delivery to the Central Nervous System: Biological Barriers, Engineering Strategies, and Therapeutic Applications

Maggie Magaw, Eshan A. Narasipura, Rachel VanKeulen‐Miller, Owen S. Fenton — 2026-11-07 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1021/acs.molpharmaceut.6c01318

Abstract Lipid nanoparticles (LNPs) have emerged as an adaptable platform for the delivery of therapeutic RNA, as demonstrated by the clinical success of mRNA vaccines and RNA interference therapies. Despite this progress, translation of RNA LNPs to diseases of the central nervous system (CNS) remains challenging due to multiple biological barriers that restrict access to neural tissue and target cells. Beyond crossing the blood-brain barrier and related CNS interfaces, further obstacles include efficient tissue distribution, cell-specific uptake, and intracellular RNA delivery. This review summarizes CNS-relevant delivery routes, key anatomical and cellular barriers limiting therapeutic access to neural tissues, major therapeutic RNA modalities, and the chemical composition and design principles of LNPs. We further discuss engineering strategies developed to overcome these obstacles and highlight emerging therapeutic applications across neuro-oncology, acute CNS injury, and neurodegenerative disorders. Collectively, RNA LNPs demonstrate potential as a versatile carrier system for neurological disease. However, further advances in delivery strategies will likely be needed to improve brain access, distribution, cell-specific targeting, and intracellular RNA delivery across diverse CNS applications.

COVID-19 vaccination uptake, safety, and clinical outcomes among patients on maintenance hemodialysis

Ahmed Atris, Issa Al Salmi, Thamreen Al Zadjali, Adham Ahmed, Muhammed Abdelmoneim Elneel — 2026-11-07 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.25259/warm_10_2026

Objectives: Patients with end-stage kidney disease (ESKD) on maintenance hemodialysis are extremely vulnerable to severe COVID-19 infection. This prospective observational cohort study aimed to investigate the level of acceptance, determinants of hesitancy, and clinical outcomes of the COVID-19 vaccine among adult ESKD patients on regular hemodialysis in Muscat, Oman. Material and Methods: A prospective observational cohort study was conducted at a renal dialysis unit (RDU) in Muscat, Oman, between December 2020 and May 2021. The study evaluated 74 outpatients on regular hemodialysis from a total unit population of 200. Participants were included if they were ≥18 years old and on maintenance hemodialysis for ≥3 months. They were categorized based on their vaccination status at the time of follow-up. Demographic characteristics, comorbidities, dialysis vintage, vaccine side effects, COVID-19 infection rates, and mortality were analyzed. Data were collected via structured questionnaires and medical record reviews. Results: Among the 74 participants actively evaluated, 44 (60%) were willing to receive the vaccine and completed the two-dose series, while 30 (40%) refused vaccination entirely. The primary reasons for refusal included fear of side effects (43.3%), family refusal (30.1%), and cultural issues (20%). Baseline characteristics, including dialysis vintage, were similar between groups. Following the first dose, 15.9% of vaccinated patients reported adverse events, primarily fever. Across the entire RDU of 200 patients, there were 84 COVID-19 infections resulting in 10 deaths. Within the specific study cohort of 74 patients, 18 contracted COVID-19. Infection rates were 20.5% (9/44) in the vaccinated group and 30.0% (9/30) in the unvaccinated group. Using the vaccinated group as the reference, unvaccinated patients had higher odds of infection (odds ratio = 1.67, 95% confidence interval: 0.57– 4.86), though this was not statistically significant ( p = 0.413). Among the 18 infected study participants, mortality was 77.8% (7/9) in the vaccinated group and 55.6% (5/9) in the unvaccinated group ( p = 0.620). Conclusion: Vaccine hesitancy remains a significant challenge among hemodialysis patients, driven primarily by fear of adverse events and misinformation. While infection rates were numerically lower in vaccinated patients, mortality was higher among vaccinated individuals who contracted the virus, though neither finding reached statistical significance. The small sample size limits the statistical power of this study. Larger prospective studies adjusting for time-at-risk and multiple confounders are necessary to definitively determine vaccine effectiveness in this vulnerable population.

The overlooked pediatric reservoir: non-declining Helicobacter pylori prevalence in children and adolescents amid falling adult rates-epidemiological, diagnostic, and preventive implications

Kanishka Uthansingh, Swarup Kumar Patnaik, Preetam Nath, Bipadabhanjan Mallick, Bibhudatta Das — 2026-11-07 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.17605/osf.io/knb58

Description Helicobacter pylori is one of the most common chronic bacterial infections worldwide and a major cause of gastric cancer. Adult prevalence has fallen in many populations as sanitation, socioeconomic conditions and access to eradication therapy have improved. A similar fall has not been shown in children and adolescents, whose prevalence remains high and varies widely by region, socioeconomic setting and age. This gap raises questions about childhood acquisition, household transmission, antimicrobial resistance, and the effectiveness of current prevention strategies. This systematic review will evaluate the global evidence on H. pylori infection in children and adolescents aged 0–18 years. It covers five domains: (i) global and regional prevalence, age-specific distribution and temporal trends; (ii) early-life acquisition, intrafamilial transmission, and environmental, familial and socioeconomic determinants; (iii) invasive and non-invasive diagnostic methods and how well they work in children; (iv) primary antimicrobial resistance, eradication regimens and treatment outcomes; (v) current and emerging prevention strategies, including household-based interventions, targeted screening, susceptibility-guided therapy and vaccine development. The review will also look at how childhood infection relates to later adult disease, including gastric atrophy, precancerous lesions and gastric cancer. Adult data will be used where needed to compare trends and prevention policies across age groups. We will search PubMed/MEDLINE, Scopus, Web of Science and, where accessible, Embase. We will also screen reference lists, relevant guidelines, and consensus statements. Eligible designs are observational studies (cross-sectional, cohort, and population-based surveys), clinical trials, and intervention studies in pediatric populations. Study selection, data extraction, and risk-of-bias assessment (using design-appropriate tools) will follow predefined criteria, and reporting will follow PRISMA 2020. We will run a meta-analysis for studies that are sufficiently similar in methods and clinical features; otherwise, we will use a structured narrative synthesis with evidence tables. The review aims to clarify whether persistent childhood infection limits long-term reductions in adult H. pylori prevalence and gastric cancer burden. It will also identify evidence gaps, particularly the lack of population-based pediatric data from low- and middle-income countries, including those in South Asia. The findings are intended to inform age-stratified prevention strategies that weigh the benefits of reducing transmission against the risks of unnecessary antibiotic exposure and resistance.

Effects of shoe longitudinal bending stiffness and runner level on Achilles tendon loading through musculoskeletal modelling

Shixin Lin, Hao Yuan, Jiaying Shao, Dongwei Liu, Mianfang Ruan — 2026-11-07 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1038/s41598-026-79775-6

Abstract Achilles tendon (AT) injuries are prevalent among runners, especially in experienced runners. This study investigated how runner level and shoe longitudinal bending stiffness (LBS) interact to influence AT loading and lower-limb biomechanics. Thirty-one male rearfoot strikers (18 experienced, 13 novice) ran at 4.17 m/s in carbon-fiber shoes of three LBS levels (T1: 10.9; T2: 17.5; T3: 33.0 Nm/rad). AT force was estimated in silico from a subject-specific musculoskeletal model with static optimization, computed as the sum of soleus and gastrocnemius muscle forces. Discrete outcomes were analysed using mixed two-way ANOVA, and waveforms using one-dimensional Statistical Parametric Mapping (SPM1D). Experienced runners produced greater peak AT force than novice runners (p=0.038, η_p^2=0.141). LBS independently elevated peak AT force (p=0.004, η_p^2=0.182) and AT impulse (p=0.006, η_p^2=0.189), driven by an LBS-related increase in peak ankle dorsiflexion (p<0.001, η_p^2=0.493) that redirected sagittal excursion proximally. Significant runner level × LBS interactions for peak knee extension moment, peak hip external-rotation angle, and transverse-plane hip ROM showed that novice runners adapted to stiffer shoes with proximal compensations, whereas experienced runners did not. Runner level and LBS independently elevate AT loading through complementary biomechanical pathways, and novice runners rely on proximal compensations to accommodate stiffer footwear. These findings support an individualized, U-shaped view of optimal LBS.

Nutritional Composition and Socio-Economic Importance of the Shea Butter Caterpillar, Cirina forda (Westwood), as a Non-Wood Forest Product in Southern Taraba State, Nigeria

Bukar M. G., Maza V. H., Segunle K. A., Oni P. I. — 2026-11-07 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.58578/kijst.v3i3.12572

Edible insects provide alternative nutrient sources and contribute to food security and rural livelihoods. This study assessed the nutritional composition and socioeconomic importance of the shea butter caterpillar, Cirina forda (Westwood), a seasonal non-wood forest product harvested from Vitellaria paradoxa trees in southern Taraba State, Nigeria. Fresh larvae were collected from selected trees across five local government areas, cleaned, and oven-dried at 60°C for 24 hours. Proximate composition was determined using AOAC (2012) methods, while minerals, vitamins, and antinutrients were analyzed spectrophotometrically. Statistical analyses employed t-tests and Duncan’s multiple range test at p ≤ .05. Socioeconomic data were collected through 200 questionnaires administered to collectors and traders. Drying significantly increased crude protein from 20.10% to 24.62% and reduced moisture from 10.74% to 6.44% (p < .05). Iron and zinc concentrations increased from 2.70 to 4.70 ppm and from 1.11 to 1.70 ppm, respectively, whereas sodium decreased from 3.63 to 3.16 ppm. Reported thiamine (vitamin B1) values declined from 97.8% to 10.9%, while retinol (vitamin A) was largely retained. Phytate and oxalate increased after drying. Among respondents, 56% were female, 39.5% were aged 18–25 years, and 48.5% belonged to households comprising 4–6 people. The socioeconomic findings indicated that C. forda contributed substantially to household livelihoods. Overall, the study identifies C. forda as a potential source of protein and minerals and an important rural livelihood resource. The processing-related changes in nutrients and antinutrients highlight the need for improved processing practices, alongside marketing interventions to support its socioeconomic value.

Multi-omics and machine learning reveal a senescence-associated lipid-metabolic macrophage phenotype driving immunosuppression and poor prognosis in colorectal cancer

Sélectionné · envoyé par email

Bo Zhao, Yonghe Liao, Shengxin Ye, Neng Jiang, 陆文奇 — 2026-11-07 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1038/s41598-026-80104-0

Tumor-associated macrophages (TAMs) are key regulators of the tumor microenvironment in colorectal cancer (CRC), yet their functional heterogeneity remains poorly understood, especially regarding the interplay between cellular senescence and lipid metabolism. We integrated multi-omics and single-cell transcriptomic data to characterize a novel macrophage subpopulation termed senescence-associated lipid-metabolic (SALM) macrophages. Using eight machine learning algorithms, a prognostic model based on SALM signatures was developed and internally evaluated via nested cross-validation in a combined TCGA–GSE39582 cohort. Spatial multiplex immunofluorescence and qPCR were employed for experimental validation. SALM macrophages exhibit elevated IL-10/STAT3 signaling, terminal differentiation, and a core gene module enriched in senescence and lipid metabolism pathways. They interact strongly with T cells via immunosuppressive ligands IL-10 and MIF. Spatial analysis confirmed p16 + CD68 + IL-10 + cell enrichment at the tumor front. The SALM-based prognostic model achieved high accuracy (AUC = 0.972), and a corresponding nomogram showed potential for personalized prognosis prediction. SALM macrophages drive immunosuppression in CRC through a senescence-lipid metabolism axis, serving as a novel prognostic biomarker and potential therapeutic target.

Concealed challenge: concealed injection of out-of-envelope process commands. Rival: counting delayed alarms as normal operation. Physical limitation: stop the bench immediately upon an interlock miss. F160 / Trustworthy Autonomous Neural Nanomanufacturing Platforms At The Limit Primary endpoint: independent quality-release assessor agreement [%]. Concealed challenge: blinded replacement of quality inspectors. Rival: false consensus from shared evaluator software. Physical limitation: no release without independently reconstructable adjudication. Release status: complete prospective research manuscripts with independent expert review pending on mathematical assumptions, semantic repetition and reproducibility. No clinical efficacy is asserted. Join the STARLI Research Community: https://zenodo.org/communities/starli-research-gate/ We do not need to know which door future generations will choose to open. We only hope that when they open it, they will find a little light to guide their way. 🌈🚪✨

DOI : https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.24027762

THE DEVICE MUST EARN ITS RELEASE An Integrated Technical Guide to Trustworthy Autonomous Neural Nanomanufacturing STARLI / YULI | Research Family 16 | Flagships F151–F160 of 200 | Author: 33 / STARLI–YULI | Research Design and Technical Integration Edition | 7 November 2026 The central question of autonomous neural nanomanufacturing is not whether an intelligent manufacturing line can produce a visually convincing neural electrode. It is whether the line can demonstrate, by evidence accessible to an independent examiner, that the fabricated object meets its declared physical and functional criteria, that its apparent efficiency was not created by quietly excluding unsuccessful attempts, that its defect assessment is not dependent on a shared mistake, and that the machine would refuse an unsafe or unqualified decision even when the optimization algorithm prefers to continue. Neural nanomanufacturing brings a remarkable variety of engineering disciplines into contact. Lithography controls geometry; material preparation establishes the initial chemical state; microscopy and electrochemical measurements create partial views of a device; statistical models infer hidden quality; control systems change recipes; safety interlocks restrict what the equipment may do; and qualified people determine whether the resulting evidence is sufficient for release. Each discipline can work well in isolation while the complete manufacturing claim remains false. This guide proposes a way to make the interfaces between them scientifically accountable. The basis for this essay is a set of ten prospective STARLI / YULI research introductions, F151 through F160. Their research questions concern autonomous recipe selection, closed-loop nanoelectrode fabrication, intelligent nanomaterial quality control, adaptive microstructure formation, AI-based defect detection, process digital twins, cross-batch electrochemical stability, energy efficiency, autonomous stopping, and trustworthy release authority. Those ten investigations are treated here as interacting duties within one nonclinical experimental program, not as ten completed demonstrations and not as proof that an integrated factory already exists. Their common physical perimeter is a controlled neural nanoelectrode manufacturing bench, with coded material lots, instrument-calibration records, machine event logs, independent inspection, and hardware-level ability to stop. Their common scientific demand is falsifiability. A device receives no special status because its manufacturer calls the controller intelligent, autonomous, quantum, or advanced. Its status comes from comparisons with an appropriate conventional line, independent measurement and a complete record of the attempts that failed. A newcomer can enter the topic through a simple thought experiment. Imagine two production benches that receive nominally similar precursor materials and seek to produce a defined pattern of neural electrodes. The first follows a fixed qualified recipe and relies on manual inspection. The second can alter processing settings when a sensor reports a deviation. After a week, the adaptive line displays more attractive device images and announces lower production energy. Have we learned that it is better? Not until we know the denominator of attempted devices, the calibration condition of the metrology, the origin of its defect labels, whether defective parts were discarded before counting, whether the energy calculation included idling and rework, and whether a safety stop would still work if the optimizing software failed. The appropriate research object is therefore not a single successful coupon. It is the documented manufacturing episode, from material receipt to independent release or justified refusal. This episode becomes the basic unit that connects the ten flagships. 1. From a collection of manufacturing improvements to a single evidence contract The ten source themes are easier to understand when placed in a causal sequence rather than in numerical order. A recipe-selection controller can improve apparent first-pass yield only if the quality standard remains stable. A feedback loop can correct patterned width only if the width instrument is calibrated and the measurement is not a disguised response to stage temperature. An image classifier can improve sensitivity to defects only if its truth labels do not themselves omit hard-to-see flaws. A digital twin can forecast process behavior only within the machinery, geometry, and materials over which it has actually been challenged. Cross-batch consistency cannot be inferred from a single measurement fixture whose own drift follows the batch schedule. Energy efficiency cannot be asserted from the energy consumed by accepted products alone. A hardware stop is not a safe stop if it merely logs an unauthorized command after that command has already taken effect. Finally, a model that estimates quality cannot appoint itself as the authority that certifies quality. These distinctions motivate a single evidence contract binding physical production, measurement, decision, and accountability. Such a contract begins by separating four identities that are often blurred: the identity of the intended design, the identity of the manufactured specimen, the identity of the instrument that observed it, and the identity of the decision procedure applied to that observation. The intended design includes revision, geometric tolerances, functional criteria, and the allowed production domain. The specimen identity includes precursor lot, substrate, machine episode, rework history, and the reason for every interruption. The instrument identity includes calibration, operator settings, software version, environmental conditions, and uncertainty. The decision identity records the threshold, model version, comparator, reviewer, and authority to accept, quarantine, or refuse. If these identities become indistinguishable, a favorable result can no longer be reconstructed. For instance, a linewidth measurement from one device cannot quietly become the release record of another, while a changed calibration cannot be treated as if all older measurements had always used it. The contract also needs a modest definition of success. Consider a proposed joint qualification yield, Y_joint = N_pass_all / N_eligible_attempts. This is a bookkeeping ratio proposed for the integrated guide, not a newly discovered physical law. The numerator includes only devices that independently satisfy all preregistered critical criteria. The denominator includes each eligible started attempt, including abandoned, reworked, damaged, unobserved, and stopped attempts, with separate disposition codes where required. The ratio prevents a production line from improving its headline yield simply by showing only its best examples. It does not replace the specific endpoints of the ten flagships, nor can a high yield override a missed safety-critical defect or a failed interlock. A complete protocol would specify what an eligible attempt means before collecting outcome data and would report other denominators, such as yield per accepted lot and qualified devices per unit time, without conflating them. The scientific virtue of this architecture is that every claimed advantage has an adversary. A fixed-recipe line with manual inspection is the recurring strong comparator in the supplied introductions. It should be treated as a serious benchmark that may win, not as an intentionally weak old-fashioned example. To compare fairly, laboratories must specify what is held equal: precursor quality, substrate area, geometry, run time, opportunities for correction, measurement access, energy accounting, and the treatment of failed attempts. Exact equality is not always physically possible. If an adaptive controller uses additional sensing or compute resources, those additions are part of the intervention and should be reported rather than concealed. The aim is to distinguish meaningful manufacturing gain from advantages created by different accounting rules. If the conventional route passes the same criteria with fewer defects, lower burden, or simpler verification, that negative result is valuable evidence about where autonomy adds no demonstrated value. 2. The specimen, the lot, and the chronology of an honest manufacturing episode A technical instruction that begins with a model is already too late. It should begin at the loading bay of the experimental bench, where material arrives with a source, batch identity, specification, and environmental history. F151 makes this issue decisive by proposing a concealed precursor material-lot replacement. The disturbance challenges an autonomous recipe selector that may have learned a narrow relationship between settings and material behavior. If the model continues to select the same recipe while the material changes, a successful result may be luck; an unsuccessful result may reveal missing state information; and a timely refusal may be exactly the correct response. The experimental record therefore needs a clear pre-process description of what the controller was allowed to know, when it learned it, and which incoming material attributes were held back during the blind challenge. Lot coding alone is not enough. The investigator should distinguish a physical batch boundary from an analytical batch boundary. A physical lot may come from a shared formulation or supplier while the images used to train the quality model are divided into nominally independent files. If images of closely related specimens appear in both development and evaluation data, the apparent prediction skill can arise from shared surface textures, lighting or provenance rather than generalizable material knowledge. A trustworthy protocol randomizes and withholds at the level at which nuisance effects are shared. For precursor substitution, that may mean independent lots; for fixture artifacts, it m

Hierarchical graph attention and memory-augmented learning for spatio-temporal network intrusion detection

Srinivasa Rao Nune, Uma Devi Moka, G. Gifta Jerith, Pattlola Srinivas, Sreelakshmi Udata — 2026-11-07 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1038/s41598-026-78200-2

Graph neural networks are increasingly used for network intrusion detection, but their reported advantages often depend on evaluation choices. We present STAR-IDS, which builds a graph from every window of ten consecutive network flows, applies two-level graph attention and a prototype memory bank, and estimates prediction uncertainty with Monte Carlo Dropout. STAR-IDS was evaluated on NF-CSE-CIC-IDS2018-v3, with real IP-address nodes and flows ordered by start time, and on the IoT dataset NF-ToN-IoT-v2, using block-wise splits that prevent leakage, temporally blocked cross-validation, five paired seeds and controlled baselines (E-GraphSAGE, GAT, GCN, GraphSAGE and a graph-free MLP) trained under identical conditions. On NF-CSE-CIC-IDS2018, STAR-IDS achieved 87.61% ± 1.22% accuracy and 86.41% macro-F1, and no difference from the baselines or ablations was statistically significant after Holm correction; on NF-ToN-IoT, the graph-free MLP was the most accurate model (81.10% vs. 77.21%). The uncertainty estimates separated erroneous from correct predictions with an AUC of 0.89, and on that dataset flagged predictions were 16–25 percentage points less accurate than auto-accepted ones. Control experiments show that shuffling flows before windowing, synthetic node identifiers and forward-in-time splits change accuracy by up to 68 percentage points.

Genetic foundations of copper stress tolerance in safflower: bridging heavy metal contamination and global warming challenges

Fawad Ali, Arif Ali, Obaid Ullah Shah, Mian Abdur Rehman Arif, Muhammad Azhar Nadeem — 2026-11-07 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1007/s00299-026-04026-x

Abstract Copper (Cu) contamination threatens agriculture, yet safflower's genetic basis for Cu tolerance remains unexplored—especially as global warming intensifies risks through nighttime warming and soil acidification. We conducted a pot experiment using 94 safflower genotypes under four copper chloride (CuCl₂) concentrations, revealing substantial variation among genotypes and treatments with trait reductions ranging from 1.75% (root-shoot ratio) (RSR) to 57.14% (dry root weight) (DRW). Correlation analysis showed positive trait relationships, while principal component analysis (PCA) grouped genotypes into three clusters. Ten genotypes consistently performed better under Cu stress, indicating strong tolerance potential for climate-resilient breeding. Genome-wide association study (GWAS) detected 180 significant marker-trait associations (MTAs) across eight traits, and gene annotation linked candidate genes to brassinosteroid signaling, ion homeostasis, metal response, and transport. Co-expression analysis revealed a tightly connected tolerance module in which AT5G01610 and AT5G14640 shared the same top co-expressed partners ( r > 0.8), including HMA4 , CAX1 , and SOS1 , suggesting coordinated roles in Cu stress tolerance. Convergent protein-protein interaction (PPI) validation confirmed these candidates as distinct hubs that are physically interacting and transcriptionally co-regulated, supporting their function as Cu tolerance modules. In silico heatmap-based expression profiling showed distinct, stress-specific transcriptional responses in Arabidopsis roots, with Cu inducing, cadmium (Cd) eliciting mixed, and (Al) aluminum repressing expression. RNA-seq analysis identified seven Cu stress tolerance genes, validated by qRT-PCR. These findings highlight promising genotypes for developing Cu-resilient safflower cultivars, while further investigation of the highly expressed genes is needed to clarify their roles under Cu stress.

Expression patterns of Slc38a4 and mmu-miR-6948-3p in a murine model of metastatic triple-negative breast cancer

Parisa Farzamfar, Zivar Salehi, Farhad Mashayekhi, Siamak Khodayari — 2026-11-07 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1038/s41598-026-76356-5

SLC38A4 (SNAT4), a system A amino acid transporter, contributes to nutrient uptake and metabolic regulation, but its role in the progression of triple-negative breast cancer (TNBC) is unclear. We examined the expression of Slc38a4 and its predicted regulatory miRNA, mmu-miR-6948-3p , in primary tumors, invasive mammary tumors, and lung metastases in a murine TNBC model. RNA-sequencing data (GSE150928) from primary mammary tumors and lung metastases were analyzed to identify differentially expressed genes (DEGs), followed by functional enrichment, protein–protein interaction (PPI) network analysis, and miRNA–mRNA target prediction. Eighteen female BALB/c mice implanted with 4T1 TNBC cells were grouped into those with primary mammary tumors and those with lung metastases. Expression of Slc38a4 and mmu-miR-6948-3p was measured by quantitative real-time PCR (qRT–PCR). Public human datasets (TCGA, MET500, DepMap) were also examined. In the RNA-seq data, Slc38a4 was upregulated in lung metastases relative to primary tumors (logFC = 3.93) and was assigned to a PPI cluster enriched for amino acid transport and vesicle trafficking. qRT–PCR confirmed higher Slc38a4 expression in lung metastases (6.23 ± 0.48 vs. 1.00 ± 0.08, P < 0.0001) and lower expression in invasive mammary tumors (0.51 ± 0.05 vs. 1.00 ± 0.08, P < 0.0001) than in primary tumors. Levels of mmu-miR-6948-3p did not differ significantly between groups. In human data, SLC38A4 mRNA was lower in breast tumors than in normal breast tissue, and DNA methylation showed no clear relationship with its expression. Slc38a4 expression depends on tissue context during TNBC progression, being lower in invasive mammary tumors and higher in lung metastases, and this pattern is not explained by changes in mmu-miR-6948-3p. Functional studies are needed to determine whether the increase in the lung drives colonization or is an adaptive response to it.

Longitudinal multicohort analysis of tissue composition and continuous malignant states in recurrent glioblastoma

Sélectionné · envoyé par email

Pengran Yu, Kaihua Cao, Zhihua Cheng, Yu Zeng, Wenchuan Zhang — 2026-11-07 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.21203/rs.3.rs-11192692/v1

Abstract Background Bulk expression changes in recurrent glioblastoma can arise from tissue composition or malignant-cell variation. We sought to distinguish these sources and evaluate the external validity of virtual perturbation. Methods This retrospective analysis integrated 84 paired patients from the Glioma Longitudinal Analysis Consortium (GLASS), 28 single-nucleus discovery pairs and 43 malignant-eligible pairs from an external single-nucleus cohort. Brain-reference deconvolution was benchmarked in 104 pseudobulks from 52 held-out donors. Patient-paired tests, frozen external hypotheses, baseline-adjusted continuous-state models and nine public KAT5-knockout cell models were evaluated separately. Results GLASS identified 1,432 differentially expressed genes. Calibrated estimates supported increased oligodendrocyte RNA weight (+ 8.25 percentage points; false discovery rate 0.00054) and decreased vascular weight (− 6.18 points; 0.0036), with concordant directions from a second method. Malignant RNA weights failed calibration. Expanded malignant programs showed no corrected average recurrence shift. Exploratory analysis identified 293 concordant gene-axis associations in the external cohort, reduced to 33 after baseline-state adjustment in both cohorts. None of 36 previously frozen gene-state hypotheses replicated. Geneformer discrimination declined from an internal area under the receiver operating characteristic curve of 0.696 to an external value of 0.486. Virtual KAT5 deletion did not outperform matched deletion controls against real knockout (P = 0.488). Conclusions Recurrent specimens show selected tissue-composition changes alongside heterogeneous malignant-state variation. The continuous-state associations remain exploratory, and the tested prediction and perturbation endpoints do not establish a therapeutic target.

Dissecting the Shared Genetic Architecture Between Major Psychiatric Disorders and Metabolic Syndrome

Shiqi Zou, Chenke Jiang, Yihong Gan, Di Yao, Jie Yao — 2026-11-07 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1186/s40246-026-01071-1

Metabolic syndrome (MetS) and psychiatric disorders frequently co-occur, suggesting shared biological and genetic mechanisms. However, the genetic architecture underlying this comorbidity remains poorly understood. We performed a large-scale genome-wide investigation to explore the shared genetic architecture between MetS and ten major psychiatric disorders, including attention deficit hyperactivity disorder (ADHD), alcohol dependence (ALC), anorexia nervosa (ANO), anxiety disorder (ANX), autism spectrum disorder (ASD), bipolar disorder (BD), major depressive disorder (MDD), obsessive-compulsive symptoms (OCS), post-traumatic stress disorder (PTSD), and schizophrenia (SCZ). Genetic correlations and genetic overlap were assessed using linkage disequilibrium score regression (LDSC), high-definition likelihood (HDL), and genetic analysis incorporating pleiotropy and annotation (GPA). For trait pairs with significant correlations and overlaps, pleiotropic variants were identified using the pleiotropic analysis under the composite null hypothesis (PLACO). Bayesian colocalization was applied to assess pleiotropic loci. Gene-level associations were assessed with the multi-marker analysis of genomic annotation (MAGMA). Tissue-specific heritability analysis (LDSC-SEG) was applied for tissue selection, followed by transcriptome-wide association analysis using joint-tissue imputation (TWAS-JTI) and fine-mapping with FOCUS to prioritize putative causal genes. Further integration analyses included gene set enrichment analysis (GSEA), single-cell RNA sequencing (scRNA-seq) analysis, protein-protein interaction (PPI) analysis, and druggability assessment to facilitate functional interpretation. We identified significant genetic correlations between MetS and seven psychiatric disorders. PLACO analysis revealed 7,622 pleiotropic SNPs, and Bayesian colocalization detected 114 pleiotropic loci. MAGMA identified 103 pleiotropic genes across five disorder pairs. Brain region-specific heritability was enriched for MDD-MetS and SCZ-MetS, particularly in seven regions. TWAS-JTI and FOCUS identified ASCC3 , VAMP2 , and FADS1 as putative causal genes for MDD-MetS, and gene SNX19 for SCZ-MetS. Functional annotations implicated these genes in synaptic transmission and lipid metabolism, with VAMP2 highlighted as a convergent molecular node. Our findings provide evidence of pleiotropic genetic effects contributing to MetS-psychiatric disorder comorbidity. This study advances understanding of shared neuro-metabolic mechanisms and highlights novel genetic targets for potential therapeutic interventions.

A Single Cell Atlas of Peripheral Blood in Friedreich Ataxia Identifies Transcriptional Correlates of Cardiomyopathy

Shweta Sahni, Vivekanand Asokachandran, Asangla Kamai, Istaq Ahmad, Zubair Bhat — 2026-11-07 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.24041062

Friedreich ataxia (FRDA) caused by biallelic Frataxin (FXN) loss is classically a neurological disorder, yet cardiomyopathy is the leading cause of mortality. To identify peripheral transcriptomic markers, this study profiled blood from FRDA patients. Single-cell RNA sequencing of PBMCs (16 patients, 15 controls) showed an expanded CD14⁺ monocyte pool, including a subpopulation with an IL-10-associated transcriptional signature. Complementary pilot whole-blood scRNA-seq (4 patients, 4 controls) suggested neutrophils with a preliminary STAT1-associated interferon/LY6Ehigh profile (higher in patients but not significant at donor level) and signs of iron dysregulation and increased oxidative stress susceptibility. Clinically, a higher neutrophil-to-lymphocyte ratio (NLR) correlated with hypertrophic cardiomyopathy, while an IL-10/STAT3-associated transcriptional score in monocytes was lower in FRDA patients with cardiomyopathy. Collectively, the study delivers the first transcriptomic atlas of the peripheral immune landscape in FRDA and identifies co-occurring monocyte IL-10-associated and neutrophil interferon-associated transcriptional programs; of these, the monocyte IL-10 signature and the neutrophil-to-lymphocyte ratio are associated with cardiomyopathy, highlighting disease heterogeneity, and nominate candidate blood-based biomarkers.

The first chromosomal-level genome assembly of Stingless bee Lepidotrigona flavibasis Cockerell, 1929 (Hymenoptera, Apidae, Meliponini)

Dahe Yang, Junpeng Ma, Changsheng Ma, Hua Wang, Kunlin Wan — 2026-11-07 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1038/s41597-026-08126-x

Abstract This is the first genome assembly of a yellow striped stingless bee species Lepidotrigona flavibasis at the chromosomal level. Specimens were collected from Yunnan Province, China, and sequenced using PacBio HiFi long-read (Sequel II), RNA-seq (transcriptome), and Hi-C technologies. The assembly was generated via de novo assembly with HiFiasm, scaffolded to chromosomes using YaHS with Hi-C data, and manually curated in Juicebox. The final 251.74 Mb assembly comprises 16 chromosomes (Contig N50 = 10.74 Mb), exhibiting high continuity and accuracy (94.6% BUSCO completeness). Hi-C interaction maps confirmed distinct chromosomal compartmentalization. The genome contained a total of 42.13 Mb of repetitive sequences, accounting for 14.90% of the entire genome. A total of 10,041 genes were annotated across the whole genome using transcriptomic (RNA-seq) evidence, among which 8, 972 genes were successfully functionally annotated from various databases. The first high-quality reference genome of Lepidotrigona constructed in this study provides a fundamental resource for the evolutionary, ecological and conservation genomics research of stingerless bees.

RBBP5 drives cancer stem cell-like characteristics by suppressing the hippo signaling pathway through WNT5A downregulation in breast cancer

Sojeong Moon, Young-Hyeon Kye, Gu Kong — 2026-11-07 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1080/15384047.2026.2756150

Background Cancer stem cells (CSCs) are a major contributor to breast cancer recurrence and therapeutic resistance. This study investigates the oncogenic role of Retinoblastoma Binding Protein 5 (RBBP5) in driving CSC-like properties in breast cancer.Methods Genetic alterations in RBBP5 and their correlation with patient survival were analyzed using multiple breast cancer cohorts, including METABRIC, TCGA, and Kaplan-Meier (KM) plotter datasets. The impact of RBBP5 on CSC-like traits was assessed through tumorsphere formation and ALDEFLUOR assays, and in vivo xenograft models. To elucidate the underlying molecular mechanism, RNA sequencing (RNA-seq) was performed.Results Elevated RBBP5 expression was significantly associated with poor prognosis in breast cancer. (METABRIC: OS, p = 0.007; DFS, p = 0.049; TCGA: OS, p < 0.001; KM plotter: RFS, p < 0.001). RBBP5 overexpression enhanced tumorsphere-forming capacity, CSC-like population and tumor-initiating cell frequency. RNA-seq results revealed an enrichment of gene sets associated with negative regulation of cell growth, invasiveness, and stem cell population in RBBP5-knockdown cells. RBBP5 knockdown upregulated WNT5A, a non-canonical Wnt ligand, leading to Hippo pathway activation and subsequent suppression of YAP signaling. Pharmacological inhibition of YAP1 abrogated RBBP5-induced CSC-like properties, while suppression of MST1/2 in RBBP5-knockdown cells restored YAP activity and CSC traits.Conclusions These findings indicate that RBBP5 promotes CSC-like properties in breast cancer by repressing WNT5A-mediated Hippo signaling. We suggest RBBP5 as a potential biomarker for poor prognosis and propose that targeting YAP or activating the Hippo pathway could represent a promising therapeutic approach for patients with RBBP5-overexpressing tumors.

Contrasting Associations of LDHB and LDHA Expression with Inflammatory Transcription in Ischemic Microglia: A Computational Signature Analysis

Zhang Meng, Li Ying, Huimin Li — 2026-11-07 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.24031758

This repository contains the processed single-cell RNA-seq data (Seurat objects), R analysis scripts, and result tables for the manuscript titled "Contrasting Associations of LDHB and LDHA Expression with Inflammatory Transcription in Ischemic Microglia: A Computational Signature Analysis". The provided data includes strictly purified microglia from three independent cohorts (GSE227651, GSE174574, and GSE267240). Using a rigorous dual-filtering strategy and Spearman rank correlation analysis, we observed contrasting associations: Ldhb expression is negatively correlated with inflammatory transcription (r = -0.1046 in MCAO-only; 95% CI [-0.1316, -0.0790]), whereas Ldha expression is positively correlated (r = 0.1922 in MCAO-only; 95% CI [0.1660, 0.2170]). These associations were detectable in the MCAO cohort but near zero or non-significant in Sham samples, which exhibited significant restriction of range. The repository includes: 01_Code/: R scripts for QC, dual-filtering, AddModuleScore scoring, AUCell scoring, and robustness checks (Bootstrap CI and Fisher r-to-z). 02_Processed_Data/: Seurat objects (.rds) containing the strictly purified microglia for all three cohorts. 03_Result_Tables/: Supplementary tables detailing gene expression rates, cohort statistics, and per-sample correlations. 04_Final_Figures_and_Tables/: Final figures (Figure 1–4 and Supplementary Figure S1) and supplementary tables (S1–S3).

Understanding the Neurobiology of Heart Rate Variability: Central, Peripheral, and Developmental Contributions in Rodents

Meyrick Kidwell — 2026-11-07 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.26686/zbgv-fb65

The study of heart rate variability has grown rapidly in popularity in the age of smart technology, however this growth at the cutting edge has progressed without the necessary foundational understanding of the complexities. Rodent modelling of HRV allows for the experimental testing of underlying mechanisms to build a strong foundation for future studies in HRV, however, current rodent experiments are limited and lacking in methodological consistency. The first chapter of this thesis will provide a theoretical overview of HRV from early foundational concepts, up to the current theoretical models, while providing a strong foundation usable to those new to the field of HRV and experts alike. Chapter two provides a data driven methodology for the study of HRV in rats, from a detailed surgical procedure through to the transparent analysis and reporting of essential parameters. Chapter three examines the contributions of the sympathetic and parasympathetic nervous system to many of the common parameters of HRV using centrally acting and peripherally isolated pharmacological blockades. Chapter four explores the central regulation of HRV through the manipulation of GABA in the infralimbic prefrontal cortex via intracerebral infusions of muscimol and bicuculine. Chapter five explores the developmental trajectory of HRV parameters in male and female rodents, starting from post-natal day four until post-natal day 60. Chapter six re-examines the results of the prior chapters and examines the existing theoretical and mechanistic models of HRV through the Lense of the new findings. Furthermore, suggestions for the next stage of testing the fundamentals of HRV are provided. Together, this thesis provides a strong argument for the re examination of rodent HRV using data-driven and cross laboratory consistency while providing the methods for this to occur.

PAQR5 links microvascular invasion, innate immune inflammatory amplification, and platinum resistance in ovarian cancer

Shimin Huang, Lijiang Xu, Jing Li, Shuting Huang, Zidan Lin — 2026-11-07 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1016/j.tranon.2026.103104

Background Platinum resistance remains the principal obstacle to improving survival in ovarian cancer. Microvascular invasion (MVI) is associated with chemoresistance and immune remodeling, but the molecular regulators connecting vascular invasion, innate immune-driven inflammatory amplification, and treatment failure remain poorly defined. Methods Three public datasets and 118 literature-curated MVI genes were integrated. Differential expression analysis followed by three-way intersection identified PAQR5 as the core candidate. Its clinical, immune-inflammatory, and pharmacological relevance was mapped using CIBERSORT, ESTIMATE, pRRophetic, and nomogram modeling, and validated by qPCR/Western blot, MTT/CCK-8, colony/wound-healing assays, flow cytometry, gamma-H2AX foci, EdU incorporation, and subcutaneous xenografts (n = 5–6 mice/group). Results PAQR5 was the only gene shared among cisplatin-resistance signatures in vitro (303 DEGs), patient tissue resistance signatures (2507 DEGs), and the MVI gene set. TCGA tumors overexpressed PAQR5 2.8-fold compared with GTEx normal tissue (P < 0.001). High PAQR5 independently predicted shortened overall survival (HR = 1.18; 95% CI 1.02–1.44; P = 0.005) and 5-year progression-free survival (HR = 1.33; 95% CI 1.09–1.63; P = 0.04). In silico, PAQR5-high tumors showed an innate immune-skewed suppressive profile, with increased M2 macrophages (+28%) and Tregs (+17%) but reduced CD8+ T cells (-31%) and NK cells (-21%; all P < 0.01). They also displayed elevated cisplatin, docetaxel, and paclitaxel IC50 values (each P < 0.0001), whereas AKT-inhibitor VIII IC50 fell 1.7-fold. Functionally, PAQR5 overexpression increased proliferation, colony formation, migration, DNA damage tolerance, and xenograft growth, whereas silencing produced mirror-image suppression and restored cisplatin-induced apoptosis. Conclusion PAQR5 appears to couple MVI with platinum resistance by coordinating an immunosuppressive inflammatory tumor microenvironment, enhanced DNA damage repair, and reduced apoptosis. These findings nominate PAQR5 as a mechanism-linked biomarker and a therapeutic entry point for targeting inflammation-associated chemoresistance in ovarian cancer.

Determination of Antioxidant Enzyme Activities among Academic and Non-Academic Staff of Federal University Wukari

Isaac John Umaru, Kerenhappuch Isaac Umaru, Ale Ebenezer Morayo, Atabo Amodu, Chakfa Nanmar — 2026-11-07 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.58578/ajcmpr.v4i1.12574

Oxidative stress arises from an imbalance between reactive oxygen species and antioxidant defenses, and occupational stress may influence this balance. This study compared superoxide dismutase (SOD), catalase (CAT), and glutathione peroxidase (GPx) activities between academic and non-academic staff at Federal University Wukari. A cross-sectional comparative study involved 60 participants, comprising 30 academic and 30 non-academic staff. Venous blood samples (5 mL) were collected, processed, and analyzed using spectrophotometric methods. Ethical approval and informed consent were obtained. Data were analyzed using SPSS version 25.0, with independent-samples t-tests used for group comparisons and statistical significance set at p < .05. Academic staff exhibited significantly higher activities of all three antioxidant enzymes. Mean SOD activity was 12.4 ± 2.1 U/mg protein among academic staff, compared with 10.1 ± 1.8 U/mg among non-academic staff (p = .003). Corresponding CAT activities were 45.6 ± 5.3 and 42.2 ± 4.9 U/mg (p = .045), while GPx activities were 8.9 ± 1.2 and 7.5 ± 1.0 U/mg (p = .008), respectively. These findings indicate differences in antioxidant enzyme activities between occupational groups, with consistently higher activities among academic staff. The study provides comparative biochemical evidence relevant to staff wellness and highlights the potential value of wellness interventions and stress management strategies tailored to occupational roles within universities.
