Mitigation of Close-Bearing Interference in Horizontal Arrays Using Frequency–Wavenumber Deconvolution

Jiaqi Chu, Guojun Xu — 2026-11-07 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.22541/authorea.15011833/v1

Close-bearing sources produce overlapping spatial responses in finite-aperture horizontal arrays, affecting amplitude-envelope estimation. This letter evaluates frequency–wavenumber Richardson–Lucy deconvolution using paired simulations of incoherent narrowband sources. At a bearing separation of 0.6°, the median normalized envelope deviation is reduced by 22.9% relative to conventional beamforming. Improvements occur at the tested separations from 0.2° to 0.8°, but reverse at 1.0° and above. With a 6 dB source-level difference, both the improvement at 0.6° and the degradation at 1.0° are more pronounced for the weaker source. Comparisons with the prescribed source envelopes confirm the same trends in normalized envelope error.

CADE: a reference-free workflow for coefficient-sensitivity analysis in bulk transcriptomic differential expression

Sélectionné · envoyé par email

Junwei Huang, Haiqing Zheng, Hongbin Jiang, Qi Wei, Xiaolei Wei — 2026-11-07 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1186/s12859-026-06756-x

Bulk differential-expression (DE) coefficients can change when disease alters cell-type abundance, but matched single-cell references are often unavailable in small or rare-disease studies. Existing marker-covariate adjustments can produce an adjusted DE table, yet they do not directly report which gene-level coefficients are stable, attenuated, amplified or representation-sensitive after composition adjustment. Coefficient-sensitivity analysis deliberately asks a narrower question than deconvolution: for each gene, how much does the reported group effect change once marker-derived composition weights are added to the model, in which direction does it change, and is that change stable to marker choice and to the coordinate representation of the weights? The output is a per-gene ranking of coefficient stability, not an estimate of cell-type fractions. We developed CADE (Composition-Aware Differential Expression), an R workflow that estimates marker-derived relative composition weights, fits paired unadjusted and composition-adjusted limma models, and computes a per-gene Composition Confounding Index (CCI). CADE reports signed coefficient-change labels, marker-dropout bootstrap rank stability (repeatedly omitting markers and re-ranking genes, to show which rankings persist), label-permutation calibration, denominator-threshold diagnostics, stabilised-denominator output and isometric log-ratio coordinate-sensitivity analysis. Genes whose classification depends on the coordinate representation of the composition weights are explicitly identified. In the controlled synthetic composition-bias benchmark, CADE preserved recovery of cell-intrinsic DE genes under composition bias, including AUROC 0.996 at 100% synthetic bias versus 0.902 for unadjusted limma. Marker-derived weights recovered PBMC ground-truth relative composition with mean r = 0.993, and a macrophage activation syndrome pseudobulk evaluation against scRNA-seq-derived composition showed mean r = 0.779, with all seven mappings that had a usable scRNA-seq reference showing p < 0.001 (of eight preset categories; Neutrophils had no reference in this ground truth). In familial hemophagocytic lymphohistiocytosis PBMCs (GSE26050; n = 11 versus 33 controls), CADE illustrated the separation of low-CCI from model-sensitive coefficients within a targeted immune, iron and ferroptosis panel. CADE provides a reference-free, reproducible workflow for characterising how sensitive bulk transcriptomic DE coefficients are to marker-derived composition adjustment. It reports coefficient stability and does not estimate absolute cell fractions. The FHL analysis is used as a small-cohort worked example, and disease-specific findings remain hypothesis-generating.

Panax notoginseng residue fertilizer drives tobacco quality enhancement via rhizosphere multi-omics cascades

Mingpeng Liu, Jing Li, Miao Dong, Hong Zhang, Yinke Li — 2026-11-07 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1016/j.indcrop.2026.124761

The potential of using traditional Chinese medicine residues as organic fertilizers to improve crop quality has attracted increasing attention, yet the underlying soil microecological mechanisms remain poorly understood. This study investigated the effects of Panax notoginseng residue composite organic fertilizer on tobacco quality formation. A field experiment was conducted with three treatments: no organic fertilizer (CK), traditional farmyard manure (CL_1), and P. notoginseng residue fertilizer (CL_2). Agronomic traits, leaf chemical compositions, and sensory qualities were evaluated, integrated with soil multi-omics analysis to characterize the rhizospheric microbial community. The results demonstrated that P. notoginseng residue fertilizer significantly optimized the quality and output value structure of tobacco. Compared with the CK soil, P. notoginseng residue fertilizer increased the yield of premium-grade leaves by 38%. Chemically, P. notoginseng residue fertilizer modulated the foliar carbon-nitrogen balance, elevating total sugars and free amino acids, while accelerating the enrichment of key secondary metabolites such as chlorogenic acid, rutin, and scopolin. These adjustments enhanced sensory scores by reducing irritation and masking off-flavors. Soil multi-omics revealed that P. notoginseng residue fertilizer elevated hydrolyzable nitrogen and available potassium, directionally enriching core functional bacteria such as Pseudoxanthomonas , Pseudomonas , and Lysobacter . In conclusion, the P. notoginseng residue fertilizer orchestrates tobacco quality enhancement by improving soil nutrient availability and recruiting specialized microorganisms, which collectively drive foliar aroma precursor biosynthesis. These findings demonstrate that recycling P. notoginseng residue into composite fertilizer is a promising strategy for achieving high-quality tobacco production and sustainable management of agricultural waste.

Artificial intelligence‐driven bioinformatics: From analytical pipelines to model‐centered biological discovery

Sélectionné · envoyé par email

Songming Chen, Wei Zhang, Luohuan Dai, Zujian Xiong, Sicen Lai — 2026-11-07 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1002/imo2.70156

Abstract Traditional bioinformatic pipelines, which focus on predefined statistical analyses, are increasingly inadequate for handling high‐dimensional, heterogeneous, and context‐rich multi‐omics, spatial, and clinical datasets. Artificial intelligence (AI) is extending modern bioinformatics from predefined analytical pipelines toward learned biological representations, intervention‐oriented modeling, and more automated discovery workflows. This review comprehensively summarizes AI advances across genomic interpretation, single‐cell and spatial transcriptomics, multi‐omics integration, structural bioinformatics, disease stratification, precision medicine, and drug discovery. We categorize underlying AI technologies, including classical machine learning, deep representation learning, generative foundation models, and executable AI agents, and highlight their complementary use cases alongside inherent limitations and failure modes. Building on rather than claiming priority for the model‐centered paradigm described in recent literature, this review contributes a cross‐domain, failure‐aware evaluation framework organized around six unified axes: representation validity, task‐specific incremental value, robustness under biological and technical distribution shift, causal support, resource requirements, and validation/governance readiness. We further discuss key technical and social barriers and hierarchically prioritize future research directions. We conclude that AI complements rather than replaces conventional bioinformatic tools, extending the capacity for hypothesis generation and mechanism‐driven validation. This review provides structured and practical guidance for robust, interpretable, and clinically translatable AI‐driven biological discovery.

A Bayesian Functional Kriging framework for spatial interpolation of PM2.5 concentration profiles

Abdul Baseer, Mohamed A. E. Khalefa, Walaa A. A. Ismael, Ijaz Hussain, Abdulkareem M. Basheer — 2026-11-07 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1038/s41598-026-78049-5

Air pollution, especially fine particulate matter (PM 2.5 ), is a major global public health problem, causing breathing difficulties, lung disease, heart problems, and premature death. Predicting PM 2.5 concentrations at unobserved locations is essential in environmental science, especially with sparse observational data. This study introduces a new Bayesian Functional Kriging (BFK) model to predict PM 2.5 concentration profiles across Pakistan’s most populous province, using MERRA-2 reanalysis data at 17 city locations across Punjab. The BFK framework combines three methodologies: Functional Data Analysis (FDA), where continuous temporal curves model discrete PM 2.5 concentration profiles through B-spline smoothing; Functional Kriging (FK), in which functional data are used for spatial prediction of curve-valued observations; and Bayesian hierarchical modeling using Markov Chain Monte Carlo (MCMC) sampling for parameter estimation. Prior empirical distributions are assumed for all parameters of interest: mean, partial sill, nugget effect, and spatial range, while posterior estimates are derived via a hybrid Gibbs-Metropolis algorithm within Gibbs sampling. Cross-validation results show that BFK is significantly better than the FK method, with RMSE and MAE differences of 30.9% (from 16.88 µg/m 3 to 11.66 µg/m 3 ) and 29.9% (from 13.03 µg/m 3 to 9.14 µg/m 3 ), respectively. In addition, we used the proposed model to estimate temporal variation in PM 2.5 concentration at five locations not sampled during data collection: Vehari, Sialkot, Attock, Khanewal, and Okara. We found that pollution is much higher in the southern areas than in the northern ones. These findings underscore BFK’s utility as a statistically principled and practically effective tool for air quality monitoring and environmental policy.

Metabolomic and Microbial Profiling of Rat Internal Organs for Postmortem Interval Estimation: A Multi-Omics Analysis

Xin Huang, Ji Chen, Han Yan, Zihang Feng, Xiaofan Zhang — 2026-11-07 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1021/acs.analchem.6c02813

Abstract Accurate postmortem interval (PMI) estimation remains a major challenge in forensic medicine. Here, we tested whether full-length 16S rRNA gene profiles of internal organs add predictive information to untargeted LC−MS/MS metabolomics for PMI estimation in rats. Metabolomics-only, microbiome-only, early-fusion, and stacking models were compared under leakage-controlled leave-one-animal-out (LOAO) validation, with parsimonious fixed-K models selecting the top 10 metabolites and top 5 microbial features within each training partition. 16S amplification failed in all 30 day-0 and 25 of 30 day-1 internal-organ samples, in both the full-length assay and an independent V3−V4 assay, indicating that the microbial signal of internal organs is effectively undetectable in the first 0−24 h; modelling therefore covered a 2−7 day window. Metabolomic profiles tracked PMI closely (LOAO RMSE 0.467−0.677 days across tissues), whereas microbiome-only models yielded substantially higher RMSEs than the metabolomics models, although their point estimates remained below the intercept-only baseline (RMSE approximately 2.0 days). The added value of multi-omics integration was limited and tissue dependent: stacking yielded a significantly lower RMSE than metabolomics alone only in the spleen after within-tissue correction, and this difference did not remain significant after correction across the full set of comparisons. All performance estimates are internal, derived from a single cohort without independent validation, and no forensic applicability is claimed; confirmation in independent animal cohorts, broader environmental conditions, and ultimately human forensic samples is required.

Exercise-Responsive Non-coding RNAs in Neurodegeneration and Aging: Molecular Mechanisms, Systemic Adaptations, and Biomarker Potential

TianLai Zhao, Bihua Chen — 2026-11-07 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1007/s10571-026-01850-3

Neurodegenerative diseases (NDDs), including Alzheimer’s disease (AD), Parkinson’s disease (PD), and multiple sclerosis (MS), are characterized by progressive neuronal degeneration, cognitive decline, and limited therapeutic options. Timely diagnosis and effective monitoring of these conditions remain major clinical challenges. Recently, non-coding RNAs (ncRNAs), such as microRNAs, long non-coding RNAs, and circRNAs, have emerged as promising molecular biomarkers due to their regulatory roles in gene expression and remarkable stability in circulating biological fluids. Accumulating evidence indicates that physical exercise can modulate ncRNA expression associated with neuroplasticity, inflammation, oxidative stress, mitochondrial function, and neuronal survival. Exercise-responsive ncRNAs are detectable in blood, cerebrospinal fluid, and extracellular vesicles, underscoring their potential as non-invasive biomarkers for disease diagnosis, prognosis, and therapeutic monitoring. In this review, we adopt a novel framework by combining preclinical and clinical evidence, implementing a reproducible literature search strategy, and applying a comparative analysis across multiple NDDs and aging contexts. This approach enables both a mechanistic and translational perspective, highlighting not just descriptive findings but also disease-specific and exercise-specific ncRNA signatures. Furthermore, emerging methodologies such as liquid biopsy and multi-omics integration are discussed to enhance the translational potential of ncRNA biomarkers. Overall, this review advances the field by providing a critical synthesis that identifies gaps, proposes mechanistic frameworks, and outlines future directions for using exercise-modulated ncRNAs in early detection, monitoring, and personalized interventions in NDDs.

Gene Co-Alterations and Survival Benefits in Ovarian Cancer Patients Receiving Platinum-Based Adjuvant Therapy: An Analysis of the Multi-Omics Approach

Pothuraju Ramudu, Aggarapu Susmitha, Gireesh Kumar Eri, Dr. P.V. Kartheek Reddy, G. Rajeswari — 2026-11-07 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.24029016

Ovarian cancer remains a formidable clinical challenge, with a high rate of recurrence and varied patient response to standard-of-care treatments, particularly platinum-based adjuvant therapy. The traditional reliance on single-biomarker analysis has proven insufficient to capture the complex biological heterogeneity of this disease. This review article explores the transformative role of a multi-omics approach integrating data from genomics, transcriptomics, proteomics, and other high-throughput platforms in unravelling the intricate molecular landscape of ovarian cancer. We summarize recent research that shows how a multi-omics approach can detect clinically important gene co-modifications, which are certain combinations of expression-level and genomic alterations that occur within a single tumor. The study focuses on how patients receiving platinum-based therapy have a higher chance of survival when these observed gene co-alterations are present. We go over the underlying molecular mechanisms that provide increased chemosensitivity, such as deficits in the accelerated DNA damage repair pathway and distinct biomarkers of the tumor microenvironment. We also discuss the bioinformatic developments and computational difficulties needed for successful multi-omics data integration. This review suggests that a multi-omics approach offers a potent new paradigm for prognostic stratification, tailored treatment selection, and the creation of next-generation predictive biomarkers, thus ushering in a new era of precision medicine for patients with ovarian cancer.

Integrated multi-omics analysis provides insights into sesame seed coat development

Pan Zeng, Shixiang Li, Xiaowen Yan, 梁军潮, Zhiqi Wang — 2026-11-07 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1186/s12870-026-10372-5

Sesame ( Sesamum indicum L.) is an important oilseed crop. The seed coat plays important roles in seed protection, maturation, and quality formation in sesame. However, its dynamic developmental process remains poorly understood. In this study, seed coat development was investigated using metabolomic, proteomic, and transcriptomic analyses. Morphological and histological observations showed a clear developmental progression from early structural formation (5–15 days after pollination, DAP), through structural consolidation (15–25 DAP), to maturation accompanied by extensive intracellular accumulation (25–40 DAP). Based on these developmental characteristics, seed coats collected at 10, 20, 30, and 40 DAP were selected for multi-omics analysis. Metabolomic analysis revealed dynamic metabolic changes, with lipid metabolism being prominent during the early development stage, whereas amino acid metabolism and secondary metabolite biosynthesis were associated with later developmental stages. Antioxidant-related pathways, particularly ascorbate and aldarate metabolism, were consistently enriched throughout seed coat development. Proteomic analysis showed that most differentially expressed proteins (DEPs) were downregulated, with enrichment patterns shifting from RNA processing and lipid biosynthesis at the early developmental stage to translation and redox metabolism at the middle developmental stage, and subsequently to energy metabolism and protein processing at the late developmental stage. Fatty acid biosynthesis was consistently enriched across all developmental stages. Transcriptomic analysis indicated that hormone signaling, carbon metabolism, and secondary metabolite biosynthesis were associated with seed coat development. Integrated metabolome-transcriptome and metabolome-proteome analyses showed glyoxylate and dicarboxylate metabolism as a metabolic pathway potentially associated with seed coat development. Furthermore, weighted gene co-expression network analysis (WGCNA) integrating transcriptomic and metabolomic data identified the green module (MEgreen), which contained candidate genes potentially associated with metabolic changes during seed coat development. This study provides an integrated multi-omics characterization of sesame seed coat development and reveals developmental stage-specific changes in metabolites, proteins, and transcripts. The identified candidate pathways and genes provide valuable resources for further investigating the molecular mechanisms underlying sesame seed coat development.

Omics Approaches in Drought-tolerant Crop Development

Saheed Adekunle Akinola, Taofeek Tope Adegboyega, Modupe Stella Ayilara, Ismail Abiola Adebayo, Rasheed Omotayo Adeyemo — 2026-11-07 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1079/9781836991632.0015

The escalation of global climate change is anticipated to result in intensely dry and hot weather. This concurrent occurrence of heat and drought stress (DS) is expected to have a growing impact on diverse agronomic features, including yield-contributing traits, phenology, and biomass of various crops. The abnormal condition also affects vital physiological traits such as photosynthetic attributes (chlorophyll fluorescence, stomatal conductance, and chlorophyll content), membrane stability, canopy temperature depression, and leaf water content. Plants employ adaptive defenses including phytohormones, osmolytes, and antioxidant systems whose unique stress responses enable identification and development of drought- and heat-resilient crops. Multi-omics integration (proteomics, metabolomics, transcriptomics) enables systematic decoding of stress-responsive biological mechanisms in crops, surpassing single-omics approaches to reveal interconnected molecular pathways critical for developing climate-resilient, drought-adapted varieties. To complement agro-management processes, there is a need for genetic modification and molecular breeding approaches that hold significant potential in this regard.

code for:A tiered multi-omics computational workflow integrating hdWGCNA and scTenifoldKnk prioritizes blood-accessible candidate markers in thyroid eye disease

Code and intermediate objects for the computational workflow described in "A tiered multi-omics computational workflow integrating hdWGCNA and scTenifoldKnk prioritizes blood-accessible candidate markers in thyroid eye disease".The workflow prioritizes blood-accessible candidate genes from multi-omics data using thyroid eye disease (TED) as a test case, via a three-tier cascade: (1) hdWGCNA-based single-cell localization of fibroblast/macrophage co-expression modules (GSE194323);(2) tissue pathogenicity evaluation by intersecting module genes with orbital tissue DEGs(GSE58331), immune-related cell death/stress genes, and secreted/exosomal genes;(3) blood accessibility verification with three machine-learning algorithms (GSE285190),plus scTenifoldKnk virtual knockout for downstream functional prioritization. β-glucan mitigates necrotizing enterocolitis-induced long-term cognitive impairment by remodeling gut microbiota and restoring tryptophan metabolite IAA-mediated gut–brain signaling

Xingdao Zhang, Haiyan Cao, Yuni Zhang, Yuan Shi — 2026-11-07 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1080/19490976.2026.2756699

Necrotizing enterocolitis (NEC) is a life-threatening gastrointestinal disorder prevalent in preterm infants, and most NEC survivors suffer from persistent cognitive deficits and neurodevelopmental disorders, with no targeted therapeutic approaches available to date. β-glucan has been documented to regulate gut microbiota, suppress inflammation, and exert neuroprotective effects, yet its efficacy and molecular mechanisms against NEC-associated cognitive dysfunction remain unclear. In this study, we established a mouse NEC model with β-glucan intervention to explore its gut–brain axis protective effects. Behavioral assessments verified that β-glucan significantly rescued NEC-induced cognitive impairments, with comparable therapeutic effects between low and high doses. Multi-omics analyses demonstrated that β-glucan effectively reversed NEC-triggered gut microbiota dysbiosis and restored disrupted tryptophan metabolism, notably elevating the level of indole-3-acetic acid (IAA). Fecal microbiota transplantation and exogenous IAA supplementation recapitulated the neuroprotective phenotypes of β-glucan, which inhibited excessive hippocampal microglial activation and neuroinflammation, and repaired impaired synaptic ultrastructure and plasticity. Further mechanistic validation confirmed that IAA mediated neuroprotection via activating the AHR/MAP2K3/p38 signaling cascade. Collectively, β-glucan alleviates NEC-related cognitive injury through modulating the gut microbiota–tryptophan metabolism–IAA axis and subsequent AHR/MAP2K3/p38 signaling activation. This study elucidates a novel gut‒brain axis regulatory mechanism of β-glucan, providing reliable preclinical evidence for the clinical prevention and treatment of long-term neurodevelopmental complications in NEC survivors.

Time-resolved transcriptomic and proteomic profiling of cisplatin-induced nephrotoxicity in mouse models

Hongchao Zhou, Fan Yang, Jingnan Huang, Huaban Liang, Mengyun Hou — 2026-11-07 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1038/s41597-026-08487-3

Kidney disease represents a significant clinical challenge, with incidence and mortality rising despite therapeutic advances such as dialysis and transplantation. Among its multifactorial causes, drug-induced nephrotoxicity, particularly from cisplatin, a widely used chemotherapeutic agent, represents a frequent and dose-limiting complication that can trigger both acute injury and progressive damage to the kidney. To address critical gaps in understanding the temporal molecular progression of this toxicity, the present study provides the first integrated time-resolved transcriptomic and proteomic atlas of the renal cortex in two complementary mouse models of cisplatin-induced nephrotoxicity: a single high-dose cisplatin (SHDC) model and a repeated low-dose cisplatin (RLDC) model. Renal cortex samples were systematically collected at various time points and subjected to integrated transcriptomic and proteomic analysis, thereby generating a multi-omics temporal dataset. This dataset comprehensively documents the dynamic molecular trajectories of cisplatin-induced renal injury from acute stress to chronic fibrosis, providing a robust multi-omics foundation for elucidating disease progression mechanisms, offering new insights into biomarkers and potential targets for early intervention in cisplatin nephrotoxicity.

Artificial Intelligence in Healthcare: A Review of Diagnostic Imaging, Drug Discovery, Personalized Medicine and Responsible Adoption in India

Alfiya Mehboob Kazi, Prof. S. K. Said, Prof. M. C. Kolse — 2026-11-07 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.24037843

Artificial Intelligence (AI) is reshaping healthcare by accelerating diagnosis, shortening parts of the drug-development cycle and making treatment decisions more individual. This review consolidates evidence from four connected areas: medical imaging, drug discovery, personalized medicine, and the ethical and regulatory conditions under which these tools can be trusted. Imaging is the most mature domain, where deep convolutional networks now detect abnormalities, triage urgent studies and improve reconstruction, while pathology, cardiology and neurology are rapidly following. In drug discovery, learning-based models help identify targets, generate candidate molecules and select trial participants, although gains are concentrated in early pipeline stages. Personalized medicine uses electronic health records and multi-omics data to estimate individual treatment response. Alongside these benefits, the paper analyses risks concerning patient privacy, dataset bias, opacity and accountability, and maps them to the Indian context of the Ayushman Bharat Digital Mission, the Digital Personal Data Protection Act 2023 and national AI strategy documents. A layered reference architecture for responsible clinical AI is proposed, together with a research gap analysis and objectives. The review concludes that healthcare AI should be judged by measurable improvement in patient outcomes, supported by prospective validation, secure data practice, fairness auditing and human oversight.

Advances and Paradigms in Modern Plant Bioinformatics: A Comprehensive Review

Pradnya N. Ghorpade — 2026-11-07 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.26479/2026.1205.03

Plant bioinformatics has undergone a paradigm shift from simple gene discovery and sequence alignment to AI-driven multi-omics integration, graph pangenomics, and precision molecular breeding. The intrinsic hyper-complexity of plant genomes—characterized by high polyploidy, extensive heterozygosity, massive repetitive element burdens, and dynamic structural variations—has mandated the invention of specialized assembly algorithms, statistically robust annotation pipelines, and high-dimensional deep learning architectures. This comprehensive review synthesizes core computational methodologies across de novo polyploid sequence assembly (Overlap-Layout-Consensus vs. De Bruijn Graphs), structural and evidence-based functional annotation, epigenomic landscape mapping (WGBS, ATAC-Seq), graph-based pangenomics, single-cell multi-omics resolution, and transcriptomic network modeling. Furthermore, we evaluate state-of-the-art transformer-based Genomic Language Models (GLMs), structural biology integrations (AlphaFold3/ESMFold), and specialized plant databases. Finally, key computational bottlenecks, infrastructure scaling considerations, and actionable computational frameworks for accelerating climate-resilient crop breeding are critically evaluated.

Quantum-resistant blockchain-assisted federated secure cancer diagnosis framework using hierarchical window pattern cognition network

Shaik Asmeen, Venkata Bhikshapathi Chenam — 2026-11-07 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1038/s41598-026-78779-6

Abstract Breast cancer is still a major cause of cancer mortality in women, and the development of efficient, reliable and explainable diagnostic systems capable of inter-hospital data sharing while maintaining privacy of data is essential. This study proposes a Hierarchical Window Pattern Cognition Network for breast cancer diagnosis using the Breast Histopathology Images (IDC Regular Patches) dataset. The workflow starts with data collection, then the Patient-Level Data Partitioning process by applying patient identifiers to avoid training-testing overlap. The framework uses CLAHE and data augmentation, image normalization and image resizing to enhance the image consistency. The network learns hierarchical histological and cellular morphological patterns by the modules: Global Histological Representation, Cellular Morphology Learning, Adaptive Tissue Fusion and Diagnostic Probability Estimation. A federated learning framework with five clients uses Federated Averaging (FedAvg) to aggregate models and share model updates securely. The security layer uses an AES-256-GCM encryption algorithm, a SHA3-512 hashing algorithm, and a mechanism simulating those of CRYSTALS-Kyber and CRYSTALS-Dilithium for cryptographic security. Record-integrity verification is supported by a local hash-chain audit log, and predictions of the model are explained with visual and feature-level explanations by using GradCAM + + and SHAP. The proposed centralized model achieved an accuracy of 94.23%, precision of 94.00%, recall of 94.47%, F1-score of 94.24%, and ROC-AUC of 0.9651. These findings highlight the promise of combining the histopathology classification, privacy-preserving federated learning, cryptographic protection, tamper-evident audit logging and explainable artificial intelligence within a unified diagnostic framework.

Alterations in Sputum and Fecal Microbiota and Their Associations With Systemic Inflammation in Patients With Acute Exacerbation of COPD: An Exploratory Cross-Sectional Study

Na Lv, Mingxuan Liu, Jing Zhang, Wei Zhang, Peizhen Li — 2026-11-07 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.23935608

Dataset Description This dataset provides processed full-length 16S rRNA gene sequencing data and supporting microbiome analysis results from an exploratory cross-sectional study comparing patients with acute exacerbation of chronic obstructive pulmonary disease (AECOPD) and healthy controls. A total of 40 participants were enrolled, including 21 patients with AECOPD and 19 healthy controls. The final microbiome dataset comprises 67 specimens, including 33 sputum samples (20 AECOPD patients and 13 healthy controls) and 34 fecal samples (18 AECOPD patients and 16 healthy controls). Among the 40 enrolled participants, 27 individuals (17 AECOPD patients and 10 healthy controls) provided both sputum and fecal specimens. All paired specimens were collected on the same day. Data Contents This Zenodo record contains the following files: sample_metadata.csv: De-identified sample-level metadata, including sample identifiers, study groups, and specimen types. Sputum_OTU_table_with_taxonomy.tsv: Processed OTU abundance table with taxonomic annotations for 33 sputum samples. Fecal_OTU_table_with_taxonomy.tsv: Processed OTU abundance table with taxonomic annotations for 34 fecal samples. QC.xlsx: Per-sample sequencing quality-control statistics, including raw, clean, and effective CCS read counts, effective-read proportions, and mean sequence lengths. MetagenomeSeq_Analysis.xlsx: Genus-level differential abundance analysis results for sputum and fecal microbiota using metagenomeSeq. Correlation_Analysis.xlsx: Complete Spearman correlation analysis results between bacterial genera and systemic inflammatory markers, including correlation coefficients, raw P values, and Benjamini-Hochberg false discovery rate (FDR)-adjusted P values. Sensitivity_Analysis.xlsx: Correlation sensitivity analysis results, including centered log-ratio (CLR) transformation, leave-one-out (LOO) analysis, and alternative prevalence-threshold analyses. README.txt: Dataset description, file information, sequencing workflow, and analysis overview. Analysis_parameters.txt: Detailed sequencing parameters, software versions, statistical methods, and analysis settings reported in the manuscript and supporting analysis results. Sequencing Data Availability The original PacBio full-length 16S rRNA gene sequencing reads have been submitted to the NCBI Sequence Read Archive (SRA). NCBI BioProject accession: PRJNA1553558 NCBI SRA Study accession: SRP751038 BioSample accessions: SAMN64546822-SAMN64546888 (67 samples) SRA Run accessions: SRR41470071-SRR41470137 (67 runs) BioProject:https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/bioproject/PRJNA1553558 SRA Study:https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/sra?term=SRP751038 The original sequencing FASTQ files are associated with the above NCBI accessions and are not duplicated in this Zenodo record. Public accessibility of the NCBI records is subject to their release status. Sequencing and Bioinformatics Analysis Full-length bacterial 16S rRNA gene sequencing targeting the V1-V9 regions was performed using the PacBio Sequel II platform. Circular consensus sequencing (CCS) reads were generated using SMRT Link v8.0. Sequence processing included barcode demultiplexing, quality filtering, primer removal, sequence length filtering, and chimera removal. High-quality sequences were clustered into operational taxonomic units (OTUs) using USEARCH v10.0 at 97% sequence similarity. Taxonomic annotation was performed against the SILVA reference database (release 138). Alpha diversity was evaluated using ACE, Chao1, Shannon, and Simpson indices. Beta diversity was assessed using binary Jaccard distances, principal coordinate analysis (PCoA), PERMANOVA, and PERMDISP. Bray-Curtis dissimilarity was additionally used for sensitivity analysis. Genus-level differential abundance was analyzed using metagenomeSeq with cumulative sum scaling (CSS) normalization and Benjamini-Hochberg multiple-testing correction. Associations between bacterial genera and systemic inflammatory markers, including C-reactive protein (CRP), interleukin-6 (IL-6), procalcitonin (PCT), and white blood cell count (WBC), were assessed using Spearman rank correlation analysis with Benjamini-Hochberg FDR correction. Downstream bioinformatics analyses were performed using the Biomarker Technologies Bioinformatics Cloud Platform. Executable analysis scripts and project-specific command files were not provided by the platform and are therefore not included in this deposit. The accompanying analysis documentation provides the methods, software versions, and parameter settings available from the final study records. Consequently, this deposit provides processed data and supporting analysis results but does not constitute a fully executable end-to-end analysis pipeline. Data Privacy and Ethics The metadata deposited in this record are de-identified and limited to sample identifiers, study groups, and specimen types. Direct personal identifiers and detailed individual clinical measurements are not included. The study was approved by the Ethics Committee of the First Hospital of Lanzhou University (approval number: LDYYLL2023-421). Data Reuse The processed OTU tables, de-identified sample metadata, sequencing quality-control statistics, and supporting statistical analysis results are provided to facilitate data inspection, interpretation, and further research. Users should cite the associated research article, the NCBI BioProject (PRJNA1553558), and this Zenodo dataset when reusing these data.

Spring phenological timing modulates intra-annual growth: evidence of a seasonal advantage for early-flushing trees

Álvaro Rubio‐Cuadrado, Isabel Cañellas, Patricia Adame, César Pérez‐Cruzado, Icíar Alberdi — 2026-11-07 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1007/s10342-026-01968-5

Abstract The timing of budburst is a key determinant of a tree’s fitness within its environment. While numerous studies have investigated the environmental drivers of phenology, less attention has been paid to how phenological traits influence individual tree performance within the forest. Here, we assessed at the individual-tree scale whether earlier budburst is associated with enhanced growth and how intra-annual growth dynamics differ between early- and late-flushing trees. We analyzed annual and intra-annual growth together with budburst phenology across the Spanish plots of the ICP Forests intensive monitoring network (Level II) representative of major forest ecosystems in Spain, which include Mediterranean and temperate forests. Annual growth data were obtained from 296 trees monitored over 28 years, while phenological observations covered 13 years and intra-annual growth 11 years for a subset of 127 trees. On average, trees with earlier budburst exhibited higher annual growth, and the relationship was positive in seven of the nine species, although it was individually significant only in Pinus sylvestris and P. pinaster , and the differences among species were not statistically conclusive. No significant relationship was found in P. halepensis , P. pinea , P. nigra , Quercus ilex , Q. suber , Q. petraea , or Fagus sylvatica ; in F. sylvatica the tendency was negative, possibly owing to its high sensitivity to late frosts. Neither leaf habit nor any single climatic or edaphic variable significantly modified the relationship, although the plots of the two species with a significant relationship all belonged to the group of cooler sites, and none of the species from the warmer and drier sites showed it. The seasonal course of growth differed between early- and late-flushing trees: the higher growth of early-flushing trees was not restricted to the onset of the growing season but extended over the whole growth period.

Preliminary exploration of sequential DPL and CO2 laser versus CO2 laser monotherapy for the prophylactic management of early postoperative linear scars: A retrospective cohort study

Jingyan Huang, Lin Lü, 俞舜, Jing Luo, Jun Yang — 2026-11-07 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1007/s10103-026-05081-2

Background and Objectives. Prophylactic management of early postoperative linear scars remains a clinical challenge. This study aimed to evaluate the clinical outcomes of sequential dye pulsed light (DPL) and fractional CO 2 laser (CO 2 laser) versus CO 2 laser monotherapy in the prevention of scar hypertrophy, and to explore potential simplified early interventions. Patients and Methods. In this exploratory retrospective study, 38 patients with early postoperative linear scars were enrolled. They were divided into a sequential DPL-CO 2 laser group and a CO 2 laser monotherapy group (19 patients each). All received six monthly prophylactic treatments. Efficacy was assessed by two independent surgeons using the Patient and Observer Scar Assessment Scale (POSAS). Results. After six treatments, no statistically significant difference was detected in the total POSAS score between the two groups ( p = 0.988, mean difference = -0.26, 95% CI: -1.66 to 1.14). Similarly, no significant differences were found in the patient (PSAS) or observer (OSAS) component scores. However, compared to baseline, both groups showed significant improvement in several key scar parameters, including vascularization, thickness, color, stiffness, and irregularity. One case of transient reactive hyperplasia in the CO 2 group was resolved with conservative management. Conclusions. Both sequential DPL-CO 2 laser therapy and CO 2 laser monotherapy were associated with favorable clinical trajectories in early postoperative linear scars over 6 months. In this exploratory, underpowered retrospective cohort, no definitive comparative conclusions could be drawn between the two regimens. Spontaneous maturation likely contributed to these outcomes. The observed parallel trends generate a hypothesis requiring proper prospective randomized evaluation to determine the true utility of CO2 laser alone as a simplified early intervention.

A statistical distinguishability-based PMU placement method for fault location problem in distribution networks

Dmytro Kaliuzhnyi, Matteo Spiller, Marco Merlo, Hamed Nafisi — 2026-11-07 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1016/j.epsr.2026.114472

This paper proposes a statistical distinguishability-driven framework for optimal placement of phasor measurement units (PMUs) in distribution networks to support machine learning (ML)-based fault location. For fault classification tasks, measurement separability is quantified using the Mahalanobis distance under Gaussian uncertainty, establishing a link to the Bayes error probability. For fault parameter regression, the framework is extended via the Fisher information matrix (FIM), where the Cramér–Rao lower bound (CRLB) characterizes the fundamental limit of estimator performance.,The PMU placement problem is formulated as a worst-case optimization of the minimum pairwise Mahalanobis distance and solved using a sequential strategy that improves the least distinguishable regions of the network. A stopping criterion based on the Bayes error bound ensures that no critically indistinguishable fault scenarios remain, providing an a priori measure of classification feasibility without model training.,After this criterion is satisfied, further PMU deployment shifts the objective from classification to parameter estimation, where additional sensors increase the Fisher information content and improve CRLB-based identifiability. This defines a two-stage placement strategy separating classification-oriented separability from regression-oriented information gain.,The approach is validated on a 20 kV distribution feeder using electromagnetic transient simulations. A Random Forest (RF) model is used as an empirical benchmark, showing strong agreement between information-theoretic metrics and observed ML performance for both classification and regression tasks.

Adaptive error management for fault-tolerant quantum computing across hardware-level suppression, system-level diagnosis, and real-time decoding

Marwa R. Donkol, Naima Kaabouch — 2026-11-07 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1007/s44464-026-00050-8

Abstract Fault-tolerant quantum computing reached a milestone in December 2024 when Google’s Willow processor demonstrated below-threshold error correction on a superconducting chip, a result since reproduced on neutral-atom and alternative superconducting platforms. Yet the classical decoders needed to sustain this performance face a scaling challenge: noise becomes increasingly structured and correlated as systems grow, while decoders must respond within microsecond coherence windows. This survey asks whether error suppression, noise diagnosis, and decoding are better treated as one adaptive control problem than as three separate research programs. Drawing on a scoping review of 146 records, it organizes the field into a three-layer taxonomy. Layer 1 (Hardware-Level Suppression) integrates protection into the physical substrate; bosonic cat qubits exponentially suppress bit-flip errors, projecting up to 90% overhead reduction versus surface codes. Layer 2 (Diagnosis) employs probabilistic models and computational intelligence to characterize error sources; neuro-fuzzy architectures and the Bias–Entropy decomposition separate hardware drift from logical faults, subject to an identifiability limit that we characterize. Layer 3 (Real-time Decoding) targets the integration of machine learning within strict latency constraints. Hardware acceleration now approaches superconducting cycle times, which shifts the case for learned decoders from speed toward accuracy under correlated noise; recent advances favor hybrid, physics-informed architectures: Neural Belief Propagation resolves degeneracy issues in quantum low-density parity-check codes by embedding topological constraints directly into the network’s loss function. These results suggest a transition toward closed-loop AI architectures that treat error management as continuous control rather than static algorithm selection.

Perceived personalization, digital FOMO, and repeat purchase intention: The boundary role of algorithmic awareness in social commerce

Pham Thai Son, NGUYEN Thi Lan Phuong — 2026-11-07 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1016/j.ssaho.2026.103942

Personalized recommendation systems and persuasive interface cues are prominent features of social-commerce platforms, but users do not respond to them uniformly. Drawing on the Stimulus–Organism–Response (SOR) framework and an interaction-oriented view of human–AI responses, this cross-sectional study examines associations among perceived personalization, perceived digital nudging cues, perceived algorithmic transparency, situational digital Fear of Missing Out (FOMO), algorithmic trust, perceived personalization accuracy, social-commerce engagement, and repeat purchase intention. Survey data from 452 users of TikTok Shop, Shopee, and Lazada in Vietnam were analyzed using PLS-SEM. Perceived personalization and nudging cues were positively associated with situational digital FOMO, while perceived personalization and perceived algorithmic transparency were positively associated with algorithmic trust and perceived personalization accuracy. FOMO, trust, and perceived accuracy were each positively associated with social-commerce engagement, and engagement was strongly associated with repeat purchase intention. Algorithmic awareness weakened the positive associations of perceived personalization with both FOMO and trust. The study therefore positions algorithmic awareness as a theoretically meaningful user-side boundary condition reflecting recognition and understanding of algorithmic personalization processes, while treating digital FOMO as a context-specific manifestation of FOMO rather than claiming a wholly new dispositional construct. Because the design is single-wave and self-reported, all paths are interpreted as associations and statistical indirect effects rather than causal mechanisms.

Prevalence and symptom severity of thrower’s shoulder in elite youth handball players

Leonard Achenbach, Anne Benning, Florian Zeman, Moritz Kaiser, Jonas Limmer — 2026-11-07 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1186/s13102-026-02199-2

Abstract Objective To identify the prevalence and severity of overuse shoulder symptoms in elite youth handball players. Methods Six-hundred and sixty (52.3% boys) elite youth handball players were followed prospectively for one season during the seasons 2016-17, 2017-18, 2018-19, and 2019-20. Players completed standardized questionnaires throughout the season and reported any overuse shoulder symptoms. The Western Ontario Shoulder Instability Index (WOSI) questionnaire was distributed five times per season. Latent class analysis (LCA) was carried out to identify different symptom patterns. Results Of 660 athletes, 148 (22.4%) (50% boys, age: 14.1 ± 0.7 years) reported shoulder problems in at least one WOSI questionnaire. The average symptom severity was 457.8 ± 352.0 points (range 4 to 1834). The questions with the highest average scores were about the lack of strength (31.3 ± 27.5), the concern that the shoulder will get worse (29.5 ± 28.5), and clicking, cracking, or snapping of the shoulder (29.3 ± 30.9). No statistically significant sex-related differences were identified across the four WOSI domains. LCA showed no discernible pattern of symptom distribution. Conclusion The prevalence of overuse shoulder symptoms is high and presents as a multi-faceted problem in elite youth handball. The WOSI questionnaire indicates symptom severity rather than distinct symptom patterns.

Institutional Career Support Participation and Placement Outcomes among Final Year Undergraduates in South India

Manikandan Kannaiyan, Catherene Julie Aarthy Charles, Preeti Slathia — 2026-11-07 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.21203/rs.3.rs-11233059/v1

Abstract Institutional career-support activities are widely used to support students’ transition from higher education to employment, but evidence linking participation in such activities to observed placement outcomes is limited. This study examined the associations of participation in institutional engagement activities (career counselling, workshops, internships, networking and professional development programmes, analysed as one set), academic performance and attendance with placement status among final-year undergraduates of their final lap at a higher education institution in South India. A census-based cross-sectional survey yielded 500 responses (response rate 89.3%). Associations were examined using chi-square and Welch tests and nested binary logistic regression, supported by diagnostic and sensitivity analyses. Overall, 88.6% of students had participated, and 90.0% had secured a job. Placement was reported by 96.2% of participants and 42.1% of non-participants. In the adjusted model, participation (OR = 17.15, 95% CI 7.05–41.73) and CGPA band (OR = 3.68 per band, 95% CI 1.56–8.70) remained associated with placement. Attendance was strongly associated with placement in the bivariate analysis, but its adjusted association was not statistically significant (OR = 2.28 per 10 percentage points, 95% CI 0.88–5.91) and was inconsistent across sensitivity analyses, reflecting its strong correlation with CGPA ( r = 0.87). Because participation was voluntary and the design was cross-sectional, the findings indicate associations rather than effects. They suggest that institutions examine barriers to participation and evaluate coordinated academic and career support using longitudinal designs.

The Necessary Non-Action: When Hesitation Becomes Correctness — and When It Becomes Failure

Rogério Figurelli — 2026-11-07 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.24038384

An apparent absence of action can denote successful restraint, an already satisfied goal, an epistemically necessary pause, an authorized refusal, a missed obligation, or a false statement of completion. These states are not interchangeable. Treating a zero-tool step as a generic failure hides correct non-action; treating a declaration of hesitation as sufficient justification protects inaction that has become avoidable, unsafe, or strategically convenient. This paper develops a conditional, evidence-relative theory of necessary non-action in tool-using language-model agents. It reconstructs a documented conceptual lineage from intention-gated computation and computable hesitation to agentic claim–state qualification, while positioning that lineage against established selective prediction, sequential decision theory, supervisory control, agentic abstention, and formal verification. The proposed framework separates goal satisfaction, action necessity, admissibility, observability, temporal opportunity, and report validity. Twelve normative principles support ten bounded propositions about no-op success, non-identifiability, obligation-sensitive idleness, reversible hesitation, deadline effects, and the impossibility of evaluating non-action from tool counts alone. A typed Non-Action Warrant is proposed as a challengeable record, not as a new security primitive or a guarantee of correctness. A limited secondary audit of a public research package of 64 controlled agent sessions confirms that zero-call records are numerous and denominator-sensitive, but cannot label them as justified or unjustified without task-state ground truth. An adversarial validation agenda specifies positive and negative controls, matched conventional baselines, explicit opportunity costs, and falsification conditions. No new live-model trials, causal treatment effects, or unique Field-specific advantage are claimed. The scientific target is a narrower question: under which stated conditions is not acting the correct operational decision, and when must hesitation stop being credited as responsible behavior?

Machine learning-driven spatio-temporal zonation of groundwater depletion using hydro-climatic trend analysis in Purulia, West Bengal

Bikash Manna, Shweta Rani — 2026-11-07 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1007/s44288-026-00822-w

Sustainable groundwater management in semi-arid regions requires a precise understanding of the complex interactions between climatic variables and subsurface storage. To address this, the primary objective of this study is to develop a robust machine learning framework for the spatio-temporal zonation of groundwater depletion across Purulia, India, by integrating 20 years (2005 to 2024) of multi-parametric hydro-climatic trends. In terms of methodology, the non-parametric Mann-Kendall test and Sen’s Slope estimator were utilized to quantify the velocity of change in Ground Water Levels (GWL), Actual Annual Rainfall (AAR), Evapo-Transpiration (ET), and Annual Soil Moisture (ASM). These dynamic trend signatures were subsequently clustered using the Density-Based Spatial Clustering of Applications with Noise (DBSCAN) algorithm to delineate three distinct hydrological regimes: high depletion, moderate depletion, and potential zones, with model validity confirmed via one-way ANOVA (F = 17,796, p < 0.001) and silhouette analysis. The key results reveal that 14.71% (920.69 km²) of the study area falls within the High Depletion Zone, exhibiting an average decline of − 0.048 m/year. Crucially, a distinct decoupling between groundwater trends and precipitation was detected in these hotspots ( r = -0.15), where depletion persists despite rising rainfall (+ 2.71 mm/year), signalling dominant anthropogenic over-extraction. From an operational perspective, this research provides a statistically validated, high-resolution diagnostic tool enabling regional planners to implement targeted artificial recharge, prioritized water budgeting, and sustainable pumping regulations in critical aquifer stress zones.

Framing Poverty as Individual vs. Group and Target Identifiability: Effects on Tolerance for Economic Inequality.

Jinri Lee — 2026-11-07 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.17605/osf.io/a7rh5

This study investigates how narrative framing of poverty—comparing an unidentifiable individual, an identifiable individual, and an unidentifiable group—influences public tolerance for economic inequality. While prior research primarily focused on wealth framing at the top of the economic ladder (e.g., Walker et al., 2021), this project shifts the focus to the bottom ("poverty") to examine distinct emotional and attributional pathways. To prevent confounding fundamental attribution errors (victim-blaming), experimental scenarios hold narrative effort constant across all conditions (depicting diligent small business owners facing closure due to mega-franchise entry). Using a 3-condition between-subjects design ($N = 300$ South Korean participants), we examine whether individual (vs. group) poverty framing increases support for redistributive policies, lowers acceptable inequality, and reduces acceptable CEO-to-worker pay ratios. Furthermore, parallel mediation analyses (Hayes’ PROCESS Macro Model 4) test whether sympathy and attribution direction jointly mediate these effects, while planned orthogonal contrast analyses evaluate whether the framing effect operates independently of the identifiable victim effect (IVE). Overall, this research provides theoretical insights into the psychological mechanisms governing public support for socioeconomic policies.

After Spirit: Fare Composition, Incumbent Pricing, and Capacity Replacement

DJ Bracken — 2026-11-07 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.24044513

Praxis Labs Economics Working Paper PE-001. Author version 1.0, dated 5 November 2026. This working paper has not been peer reviewed. Spirit Airlines ceased operations on May 2, 2026. Using monthly U.S. ticket data and operated capacity, we distinguish the loss of a cheap seller from incumbent pricing and seat replacement. On 109 exposed domestic airport pairs, the observed mean nonstop one-way ticket amount rose from $203.22 in January-February to $261.88 in June-July. An exact baseline-first decomposition assigns $40.41 of the $58.66 increase to a baseline Spirit-removal benchmark; this is accounting, not causal attribution. The original fixed-cell incumbent-fare association is 11.1%. An exploratory entropy-balanced comparison gives 5.7%, with substantial uncertainty and narrower population coverage. A within-carrier, route, direction and month comparison of short- and long-booking fares gives an imprecise 3.8% premium change. We supply a separate positive-semidefinite airport-score covariance and formal projection intervals allowing bounded counterfactual drift. Under airport dependence, the original fare interval includes zero when monthly post-baseline drift may be one quarter of the largest pre-baseline drift, accounting for uncertainty in both. Rivals' observed seat increases replace 27.2% and 39.9% of baseline Spirit capacity in June and July; a longer-history seasonal model estimates a 12.6-point total-capacity shortfall. The evidence documents fewer cheap options and incomplete short-run capacity replacement, while leaving the precise causal incumbent-fare response unresolved. It does not identify the welfare effect of the blocked merger. DJ Bracken directed the study, evaluated the evidence and results, and edited and approved the final manuscript. As sole author, he takes responsibility for its conclusions. OpenAI ChatGPT research agents provided substantial assistance with study design, source verification, data collection and cleaning, statistical programming and analysis, computational checks, interpretation, drafting, and figures. Research data and participants: The research uses public aggregate airline records and no recruited participants or identifiable passenger records.

TOURIST HETEROGENEITY IN FOOD DESTINATION IMAGE: GENDER, AGE AND LENGTH OF STAY DIFFERENCES, MALAYSIA

MOHD SYAIFUDDIN MOHD ALIAS, Hashim Fadzil Ariffin, Zaharah Mohamed Rani, Mazarina Devi, DAMIATI — 2026-11-07 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.55197/qjssh.v7si5.1762

This study examines whether food destination image perceptions differ across selected dimensions of tourist heterogeneity, specifically gender, age, and length of stay in the Malaysian food tourism context. A quantitative, descriptive and cross-sectional survey design was employed using data from 653 tourists who participated in food-related tourism activities in Malaysia. Independent samples t-tests and Welch’s analysis of variance (ANOVA), followed by Games-Howell post-hoc comparisons, were used by using SPSS to examine differences in food destination image across tourist groups. Effect sizes were also reported to assess the practical magnitude of statistically significant differences. The findings indicate statistically significant differences in food destination image across gender, age, and length of stay groups. Male respondents reported higher food destination image scores than female respondents, although the magnitude of the difference was small. The findings suggest that destination management organisations and food tourism marketers should recognise tourist heterogeneity when developing destination communication and experience strategies. The study provides empirical evidence that food destination image perceptions are not necessarily uniform across tourist groups within the Malaysian food tourism context. By considering both statistical significance and effect size, the study offers a more cautious understanding of tourist heterogeneity in food destination image.

Universal design as a framework for enhancing tourist friendly and well being oriented urban spaces in Luxor Egypt

Menna Allah Salah Refai, Zienab El-gamily — 2026-11-07 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1007/s44327-026-00406-w

Urban spaces in heritage tourist cities play a central role in shaping visitors’ experience and supporting residents’ quality of life. In Luxor, Egypt, the performance of these spaces is strongly influenced by accessibility, environmental comfort, safety, and cultural legibility. This study investigates universal design as an integrative framework to enhance urban well-being and improve tourist experiences in heritage urban environments. A mixed-methods approach was employed, combining a systematic literature review, analysis of international best practices, and empirical fieldwork. Primary data were collected through questionnaire surveys administered to 339 participants (55% visitors, 40% residents, 5% professionals) across two key urban areas: Railway Station Square and the surroundings of Luxor Temple, using a five-point Likert scale. Descriptive statistical analysis was applied to the survey data, including frequency distributions and mean score calculations. Independent-samples t-tests were used to compare perceptions between residents and tourists. Observational data were interpreted using universal design indicators. The findings reveal significant deficiencies: approximately 95% of respondents confirmed the lack of accessible infrastructure for all users, 88.2% reported the absence of adequate seating and shading, and approximately 82.4% considered street surfaces unsafe for movement. These deficiencies negatively influence perceived comfort, ease of movement, and overall user satisfaction. This study concludes that integrating universal design principles into heritage urban planning can substantially improve urban well-being and enhance the quality of the tourist experience, while supporting inclusive and sustainable development in culturally sensitive cities. Universal design improves accessibility and perceived urban well-being in heritage tourist cities. Empirical assessment of public spaces in Luxor reveals critical accessibility and comfort gaps. Findings support integrating inclusive design principles into heritage urban development strategies.

Probabilistic redshift estimation of undetected galaxies from multi-band background light maps

Shangrong Li, Hendrik Hildebrandt, Ludovic Van Waerbeke — 2026-11-07 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1051/0004-6361/202662029

Accurate knowledge of the redshift distributions of undetected galaxy populations is essential for extracting the cosmological information encoded in the cosmic optical background. We present the first framework for estimating these distributions directly from multi-band maps of unresolved background light, using conditional normalising flows trained on realistic image simulations. For imaging quality comparable to that expected from the survey and the Vera C. Rubin Observatory Legacy Survey of Space and Time (LSST), our model achieves sub-per cent accuracy in both the mean and standard deviation of the flux-weighted redshift distributions when the training and target samples are statistically well matched. Furthermore, the network proves highly resilient to incomplete photometric coverage when combined with tailored data imputation strategies. To address the challenge of statistical mismatches between simulations and real observations, we propose incorporating observational quantities into the target variables, providing a built-in diagnostic to assess model reliability and uncertainty from the predictions themselves. We also demonstrate the feasibility of map-based tomographic analyses, showing that the model retains sufficient pixel-level detail to recover redshift distributions for tomographically defined subsamples with near sub-per cent accuracy across all bins.
