Supplementary near- and above-rated OpenFAST simulation archive for the NREL 5-MW/OC4-DeepCwind floating wind turbine - Part 2 of 4

Luana Gurnari, Pasquale Filianoti, Sergio Mario Camporeale — 2026-11-07 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.22943450

Supplementary OpenFAST Simulation Dataset for the NREL 5-MW/OC4-DeepCwind Floating Wind Turbine — Part 2 of 4 Overview This record constitutes Part 2 of 4 of a supplementary OpenFAST dataset for the NREL 5-MW reference wind turbine mounted on the OC4-DeepCwind semisubmersible floating platform. The four-part collection extends the previously published benchmark dataset with near-rated and above-rated wind-speed classes used in the companion research study investigating wave-induced changes in mean generated power and structural-load variability. The supplementary simulations provide additional operating conditions and independent stochastic realizations for assessing the dependence of wave-induced responses on wind-speed regime and the reproducibility of the numerical results. All aggregate simulation counts and archive counts reported in this description refer to the complete four-part collection unless explicitly attributed to Part 2. Contents of Part 2 This record contains four losslessly compressed ZIP/DEFLATE archives, with a combined size of approximately 48.32 GB, comprising: First stochastic realization — 12 and 13 m/s seed1_V12p0_part01.zip seed1_V12p0_part02.zip seed1_V13p0_part01.zip seed1_V13p0_part02.zip The four archives contain wave-forced OpenFAST simulation outputs from the first stochastic realization at 12 and 13 m/s. Each wind-speed class comprises 175 qualified Class-A sea-state conditions, corresponding to 350 expected wave-forced time-history files across these two wind-speed classes. The corresponding second stochastic realizations at 12 and 13 m/s are provided in Part 4. Together, these parts enable paired-seed assessment of wave-induced power and load responses at these wind speeds. The following documentation and reference files are available in Part 1 (DOI: https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.22895713): README.md — dataset description and usage information. FILE_MANIFEST.csv — inventory of the 19 compressed archives across the full four-part collection. SHA256SUMS.txt — checksums for all 19 archives. still_water_TEST0_baselines.zip — 11 still-water baseline simulations. The file-count expectation reflects the simulation inventory, not an independent inspection of the compressed archives on Zenodo. Numerical model and environmental conditions The simulations were performed using OpenFAST v4.1.2, with the ROSCO controller in the original tuning configuration adopted for the study. The nacelle-velocity proportional feedback gain was set to Fl_Kp = 0. The numerical configuration considers: Steady, spatially uniform wind inflow. Irregular, unidirectional JONSWAP waves aligned with the wind direction. First-order wave loading, with second-order wave-force QTF contributions disabled. Simulation time step DT = 0.0125 s. Output time step DTout = 0.0125 s. The dataset represents the specified turbine-platform-controller configuration. The use of uniform wind and first-order wave loading should be considered when interpreting the results and their applicability to more general operating environments. Complete four-part dataset Across all four linked Zenodo records, the supplementary collection contains 1,463 wave-forced OpenFAST time-history files and 11 still-water baseline files, corresponding to 1,474 source output files. For wind speeds of 10.5, 11.5, 12 and 13 m/s, the collection contains 175 qualified Class-A sea states at each wind speed, with two independent stochastic realizations for each sea-state/wind-speed combination. This provides: 700 matched sea-state/wind-speed conditions. Two stochastic wave realizations per condition. 1,400 wave-forced simulation outputs across the two seeds. The above-rated diagnostic extensions contain: 32 valid single-seed simulations at 14 m/s. 31 valid single-seed simulations at 15 m/s. These latter extensions are diagnostic datasets and do not support a two-seed statistical robustness assessment. The 11 still-water simulations provide wind-speed-specific reference conditions for evaluating wave-induced incremental responses. Data format and file naming The simulation time histories are distributed as OpenFAST .out files within losslessly compressed archives. Wave-forced output filenames follow the convention: TEST_Hs<Hs>_Tp<Tp>_Seed-<seed>_Hwind<V> where: Hs is the significant wave height in metres. Tp is the spectral peak period in seconds. seed is the integer random seed used for wave generation. V is the nominal wind speed in metres per second. For matched stochastic pairs, the nominal wind speed and sea-state parameters are retained while the wave seed is changed. Archive organization and verification The complete dataset is distributed across 19 compressed archives hosted in four linked Zenodo records: Zenodo part Archive contents Number of archives Part 1 Seed 1 at 10.5 and 11.5 m/s; 14 and 15 m/s diagnostic extensions; still-water baselines 7 Part 2 Seed 1 at 12 and 13 m/s 4 Part 3 Seed 2 at 10.5 and 11.5 m/s 4 Part 4 Seed 2 at 12 and 13 m/s 4 Total Complete supplementary collection 19 Four archives use 7z/LZMA2 solid compression. The remaining 15 archives use ZIP/DEFLATE compression, including the still-water baseline archive. The SHA256SUMS.txt file, available in Part 1 (DOI: https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.22895713), provides a checksum for every compressed archive across the complete collection. Users are encouraged to verify checksums after downloading and before extracting or processing the OpenFAST outputs. Relationship to the previously published dataset This collection supplements the previously published qualified and traceable OpenFAST benchmark dataset: Base dataset — Version 2.0.0 DOI: https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.21875701 The base dataset provides the underlying 7–11 m/s simulation campaign, including the physical qualification criteria, numerical-integrity screening procedures, input files and data provenance. The supplementary collection should be used together with that dataset when reproducing the complete numerical analysis reported in the companion scientific publication. Companion loading and validation software DOI: https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.21829605 The software record provides associated tools for processing and validating the OpenFAST simulation outputs. Scope and limitations The supplementary dataset is intended to support reproducibility and further investigation of wave-induced changes in power production and structural loading for the specified NREL 5-MW/OC4-DeepCwind configuration. The simulations do not include atmospheric turbulence, directional wave spreading, wind–wave misalignment or second-order wave-force QTF contributions. The two-seed replication supports assessment of sensitivity to the investigated stochastic wave realizations but does not constitute a complete uncertainty quantification or experimental validation. The results should therefore not be interpreted as universally transferable to other turbine ratings, floating-platform designs, controller configurations or environmental conditions without additional verification. Related records Base OpenFAST dataset: https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.21875701 Companion software: https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.21829605 Supplementary dataset — Part 1 of 4 (published): https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.22895713 Supplementary dataset — Part 2 of 4 (this record): https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.22943451 Supplementary dataset — Part 3 of 4: https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.22943551 Supplementary dataset — Part 4 of 4: https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.22943555 Parts 1–4 collectively constitute the complete supplementary dataset. The four records should be used together when reproducing the analyses involving both stochastic realizations.

Meta-analysis with JASP, Part I: Classical approaches

Frantis̆ek Bartos̆, Eric‐Jan Wagenmakers, Wolfgang Viechtbauer — 2026-11-06 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1017/rsm.2026.10127

Meta-analyses play a crucial part in empirical science, enabling researchers to synthesize evidence across studies and draw more precise and generalizable conclusions. Despite their importance, access to advanced meta-analytic methodology is often limited to scientists and students with considerable expertise in computer programming. To lower the barrier for adoption, we have developed the Meta-Analysis module in JASP (https://jasp-stats.org/), a free and open-source software for statistical analyses. The module offers standard and advanced meta-analytic techniques through an easy-to-use graphical user interface, allowing researchers with diverse technical backgrounds to conduct state-of-the-art analyses. This manuscript presents an overview of the meta-analytic tools implemented in the module and showcases how JASP supports a meta-analytic practice that is rigorous, relevant, and reproducible. Tutorial videos accompany the examples presented in this manuscript.

Temporal dynamics of antigen-specific T cell expansion in primary SARS-CoV-2 infection

Sélectionné · envoyé par email

Andrew G. T. Pyo, Adlina Chan, Joshua Rosenheim, Clare E Thakker, Lucy C. K. Bell — 2026-11-06 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1126/sciadv.aec4195

Abstract Quantifying T cell response during primary infection in humans is crucial for understanding adaptive immunity. Leveraging a controlled human challenge to SARS-CoV-2, we characterized antigen-specific T cell response within and across individuals. Notably, individual clones reached similar maximum frequencies despite differences in the timing of their peak expansion. Mathematical modeling showed that this observation is consistent with precursor frequency, but not TCR signal strength, as the source of inter-clonal variability. Single-cell profiling revealed distinct temporal programs for CD4 + and CD8 + T cells, with CD4 + cells expanding earlier but contracting to a lower frequency. Clones with similar receptors, likely recognizing the same antigen, expanded at similar times. Together, these findings highlight how clone-intrinsic properties such as precursor frequency and lineage shape T cell clonal kinetics. These insights provide a quantitative framework for understanding T cell response in humans, with implications for vaccine design.

CD8+ T cells and humoral immunity may mitigate the effects of antibody-dependent enhancement: implications for dengue vaccine design

Sélectionné · envoyé par email

Riley S Drake, Hasan Ahmed, Anmol Chandele, Rafi Ahmed, Kaja Murali‐Krishna — 2026-11-06 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1128/jvi.00941-26

ABSTRACT Dengue vaccine development is complicated by antibody-dependent enhancement (ADE)—a phenomenon in which antibodies paradoxically boost viral growth. Signals of immune-mediated enhancement were observed in the CYD-TDV (Dengvaxia) vaccine trials, but have not yet been reported for trials of TAK-003 (Qdenga). To better understand how prior immunity influences dengue infection outcomes, we developed a mechanistic within-host model of acute dengue infection dynamics that incorporates ADE as well as humoral and CD8+ T-cell immunity. Model simulations illustrate a potentially very important difference between enhancement by antibody, enhancement by humoral immunity, and enhanced severity of secondary infection. Model results also suggest that recipients of CYD-TDV may have been more likely to experience immune-mediated enhancement because of its yellow fever virus backbone and corresponding lack of dengue-specific CD8+ T-cell epitopes. The model provides a possible explanation for differences in reported outcomes of dengue vaccine trials and suggests incorporating DENV backbones from all four serotypes as a potentially promising way forward in the design of future dengue vaccines. IMPORTANCE Vaccines are typically designed to elicit humoral (antibody-mediated) immunity, and antibody titers are commonly used as correlates of protection. Because CD8+ T-cell responses are more challenging to measure, they are not typically used as correlates of protection and have received comparatively less attention. For viruses such as dengue, where antibodies may increase viral replication and thus the risk of severe disease through antibody-dependent enhancement, it is necessary to understand how humoral and cellular immunity interact during infection. Our model suggests that low antibody levels can enhance viral replication but that this effect can be mitigated by humoral and CD8+ T-cell immunity. This result offers a hypothesis to explain the observed differences in outcomes between the CYD-TDV and TAK-003 trials and suggests that evaluating CD8+ T-cell responses alongside antibody titers may be important for designing and assessing the safety and efficacy of future dengue vaccines.

Blame attribution in authoritarianism: the public’s response to three safety crises in China

Dehua Sun — 2026-11-06 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1017/psrm.2026.10133

Abstract Citizens rely on information to evaluate the government, but their sources are limited in authoritarian regimes. This study shows that citizens can learn from extraordinary events, such as public safety incidents, which are socially salient, highly visible, and clearly attributable. When the state bears causal responsibility for a safety failure, political trust declines under both a controversial and an uncontroversial response. Using survey data that cover three national crises in contemporary China, the analysis compares respondents in the weeks immediately before and after the incidents. Trust in government declined following the 2011 bullet train crash and the 2018 vaccine scandal, but not following the 2008 Wenchuan earthquake. Further analysis finds no increase in perceived corruption, while perceptions of free expression declined after the train crash but not after the earthquake, consistent with dissatisfaction over information control. The findings have implications for authoritarian accountability and regime stability.

Characterization of the urinary DNA virome of hematopoietic stem cell transplant recipient and healthy cynomolgus macaques

Helena Vogel, Tristan T Neal, Helen L. Wu, Kaitlyn A. Kukula, Paul Kievit — 2026-11-06 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1128/jvi.00718-26

ABSTRACT BK polyomavirus (BKPyV) and chronic latent DNA virus infections frequently reactivate after hematopoietic stem cell transplantation (HSCT), where they contribute to major complications in humans with limited options for prevention or treatment. Mauritian cynomolgus macaques (MCMs; Macaca fascicularis ) serve as an important animal model for transplantation research due to their limited major histocompatibility complex diversity. Similar viral reactivations and disease complications occur in MCMs and humans post-transplantation, including polyomavirus-associated hemorrhagic cystitis and nephropathy. To define the polyomaviruses associated with these outcomes and characterize the broader urinary DNA virome, we sequenced rolling circle-amplified urine DNA from healthy and HSCT recipient cynomolgus macaques, allowing for comprehensive genomic characterization of the detected polyomaviruses. De novo assembly and annotation identified three polyomaviruses with apparent specific host tropism to cynomolgus macaques: Macaca fascicularis polyomavirus 2 (MafaPyV2) and Macaca fascicularis polyomavirus 3 (MafaPyV3), which are closely related to human polyomaviruses, and a newly identified strain of simian virus 40 (SV40 type IIB). All three viruses were detected in both HSCT recipients and healthy animals, but were shed at significantly higher relative loads in HSCT recipients. Multiple polyomaviruses were frequently detected within individual hosts, and all three were identified in HSCT recipients that developed urologic disease. Together, these findings further characterize polyomavirus diversity, shedding, and disease associations in hematopoietic stem cell–transplanted Mauritian cynomolgus macaques. IMPORTANCE Reactivation of latent viruses after hematopoietic stem cell transplantation is a major cause of illness in transplant recipients, yet options for prevention and treatment remain limited. Progress has been hindered by the lack of animal models that accurately reflect human disease. In this study, we show that Mauritian cynomolgus macaques harbor multiple polyomaviruses that reactivate at high levels following transplantation and are associated with urinary tract disease, closely paralleling complications seen in humans. We define the genetic identity of these viruses and demonstrate that immunosuppression strongly enhances viral shedding and disease severity. By establishing clear similarities between viral reactivation in macaques and humans, this work validates the Mauritian cynomolgus macaque transplantation model as a powerful platform for studying virus-driven disease mechanisms and testing antiviral therapies and vaccines relevant to human transplantation.

GraphPepTox: a structure-aware pre-trained framework for peptide toxicity prediction using geometric graph transformer and LoRA-based fine-tuning

Soumyadeep Bhaduri, Debraj Das, PRALAY MITRA — 2026-11-06 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1186/s12859-026-06744-1

ABSTRACT Peptides are widely used as potential therapeutic agents in drug discovery and biotechnology because they are specific, effective, and relatively inexpensive to produce. They are used in drug development, vaccines, and antimicrobial treatments. However, peptide toxicity remains a major concern as it offers unwanted toxic consequences, such as membrane rupture, haemolysis, tissue damage and adverse immunological response. Early detection of toxic peptide candidates is vital for the development of safe and effective therapies. Current computational methods for predicting peptide toxicity are largely based on hand-crafted sequence descriptors or sequence-only deep learning architectures that may not fully account for the underlying 3-dimensional structural determinants of peptide toxicity. We introduce GraphTox, a structure-aware geometric deep learning framework which combines self-supervised graph representation learning with hierarchical structural modelling to accurately predict peptide toxicity. Our framework learns geometry-aware embeddings from peptide structural graphs via self-supervised masked residue reconstruction, based on a Masked Graph Autoencoder (MGAE) built on a Geometric Graph Transformer (GGT) encoder. The pretrained structural representations are cross fused via a multi-scale U-Net architecture to capture both local residue-level interactions and global conformational patterns associated with peptide toxicity. GraphTox explicitly models spatial relationships between residues, thereby efficiently capturing structural aspects that are generally neglected by sequence-based predictors, such as residue clustering, hydrophobic interactions and electrostatic organization. On benchmark datasets our framework shows superior performance and interpretability over the existing state-of-the-art methods. Our hybrid hierarchical structural modelling framework is a superior computational platform to improve the prediction of peptide toxicity and expedite the creation of safer peptide therapies. https://github.com/debraj-55555/GraphTox

Determinants of persistent COVID-19 vaccine hesitancy: a preliminary study among healthcare providers in Lubumbashi, DR Congo

Peniel Masele-Kyaite, Marc Ilunga-Kasambayi, Philippe Mulenga-Cilundika — 2026-11-06 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1186/s13104-026-08110-z

Abstract Introduction : Vaccine hesitancy is one of the ten threats to global health. In DR Congo, COVID-19 vaccine hesitancy among healthcare providers has resulted in one of the lowest vaccination coverage rates in the world. In 2025, four years after the rollout of vaccines, the persistence of this mistrust remains poorly documented. This study aims to identify the determinants of persistent hesitancy among healthcare providers in the city of Lubumbashi. Methods : A cross-sectional analytical study was conducted via an online questionnaire (from 5 to 9 May 2025) among healthcare providers in the city of Lubumbashi, DR Congo. The reasons for hesitancy were analysed via flat sorting, followed by logistic regression to identify the factors independently associated with vaccine hesitancy. Results : Amongthe 86 participants (sex ratio M/F: 3.1; 86.1% doctors), the median age and duration of experience were 28 and 2 years, respectively. The vaccine hesitancy rate was 80.23% (n=69). The main reasons for hesitancy included mistrust of the speed of clinical trials (83.72%), low perception of individual risk of developing a severe form of the disease (83.72%) and no history of COVID-19 (81.40%). In the multivariate analysis, two factors were independently associated with vaccine hesitancy: geopolitical influence and limited professional experience (≤ 2 years). Conclusion : COVID-19 Vaccine hesitancy remains prevalent among healthcare professionals in Lubumbashi. In this exploratory study, geopolitical issues and limited professional experience were independently associated with this hesitancy. These findings suggest the potential value of transparent, context-specific institutional communication and targeted educational support for less experienced healthcare providers, which hospital administrators should consider strengthening.

Model-based evaluation of an infant HPV prophylactic vaccination program in Nigeria

Sélectionné · envoyé par email

Robyn M. Stuart, Ifeoma Umeh, Christopher John Gill, Jamie A A Cohen — 2026-11-06 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1186/s12879-026-14586-8

Abstract Background Nigeria introduced single-dose HPV vaccination for girls aged 9–14 in 2023, reaching approximately 60% aggregate coverage by 2025. While an important achievement, this remains short of the 90% coverage called for under the WHO’s 90-70-90 cervical cancer elimination targets, and further scale-up depends on reaching out-of-school girls. In this study we used a model of HPV transmission in Nigeria to quantify the residual cervical cancer burden under continued adolescent delivery, assess how additional screening scale-up and equity assumptions modify it, and identify the effective coverage requirements for an infant HPV vaccination program to be non-inferior to the existing adolescent strategy. Methods We used HPVsim, an agent-based microsimulation calibrated to Nigerian sexual behavior, HPV prevalence, and cervical cancer incidence, to project new cervical cancer cases and age-standardized incidence over 2025–2100 under scenarios which vary vaccination delivery mode (status-quo adolescent, WHO 90-70-90 adolescent scale-up, and 60/75/90% infant delivery), screening coverage (opportunistic 15% vs. scaled 70% aggregate), and the degree to which primary school completion correlated with vaccination (odds ratio 1 vs. 5) and with screening (odds ratio 1 vs. 3). Infant delivery scenarios were run at hypothetical effective-vaccine-efficacy-at-exposure values of 50%, 70%, and 95% — scenario inputs bracketing the plausible waning range, not evidence-based estimates of infant vaccine performance (no HPV vaccine trial has enrolled anyone below age 10). Each scenario was run at 50 replicates (10 calibration draws × 5 seeds); reported outcomes are the median across replicates and uncertainty ranges are interquartile (25th–75th percentile). Results Continuation of Nigeria’s current adolescent program is projected to avert 1.82 million cervical cancer cases (IQR 1.65–1.90 million; 95% UI 1.39–2.02 million) over 2025–2100 (a 53% reduction relative to no vaccination, IQR 51–55%; 95% UI 46–58%), with age-standardized incidence falling from 19.7 to 7.8 per 100,000 by 2100. However, 82% of the near-term (2025–2075) burden is projected to fall on the pre-2015 birth cohort, i.e. women already beyond the vaccination target age, leaving approximately 945,000 residual cases (IQR 905K–1.00M; 95% UI 835K–1.14M) in this cohort. Scaling up screening to WHO targets was estimated to avert a further ∼387K cases (IQR 331–424K; 95% UI 269–515K), roughly 25% of the residual burden; results were about 6–8% better if the scale-up instead reached all women equitably regardless of education. Under a hypothetical scenario of infant vaccination at 90% coverage and 95% effective vaccine efficacy at exposure (matching adolescent-age performance), outcomes were comparable to full WHO adolescent scale-up (49% vs. 54% reduction in vaccine-targetable cohort cancers), but under a hypothetical 50% effective efficacy at Nigeria’s DTP3-anchored 62% coverage the strategy averted only a fraction of these cancers. Conclusions The near-term cervical cancer burden in Nigeria is dominated by cohorts vaccination cannot reach; scaling screening for them is the more consequential lever over the coming three decades and rests on established evidence. Findings on infant HPV delivery are exploratory and hypothesis-generating: they depend on three unverified empirical prerequisites – infant HPV vaccine immunogenicity in the first months of life, durability of protection across the ∼15–25-year interval to peak HPV exposure, and coverage achievable through Nigeria’s routine childhood immunization platform – and should not be read as a basis for any policy recommendation contingent on infant HPV delivery until those prerequisites are established.

Level of immune response to polio vaccines among fully vaccinated children aged 1-10 years in Maniema and Ituri provinces, Democratic Republic of Congo : a cross- sectional survey

Bienvenu Kuyangisa Boloko, Mireille Nganga Nkanga, Aurore Beia Mbombo, Clarisse Kwessena, Audace Kilolo — 2026-11-06 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.21203/rs.3.rs-11066795/v1

Abstract Introduction: The Democratic Republic of the Congo (DRC) bears a great number of cases of poliovirus. The study aimed to assess the level of immune response to polio vaccines among fully vaccinated children aged 1–10 yeas. Methods This cross-sectional study was conducted in two provinces of the DRC and included 348 children. Results The mean age was 4.7years ± 2.7 and the median age was 5 years with extremes of 1 to 10 years. The vaccine failure rate was 56.9% for girls and 43.1% for boys. This rate was 59.3% in the Maniema site and 53.5% in the Ituri site. The difference was not statistically significant (p = 0.278). Considering the age groups, the vaccination failure rate was respectively 59.4% in children aged between 5 and 54.5% in those under 5 years of age. There was no statistically significant difference (p = 0.354). Conclusion This study showed a significant rate of vaccine failure among fully vaccinated children. This result requires further studies to investigate the factors associated with this failure.

Continuous, Size-Based Purification of Virus-like Particles Using Electro-Viscoelastic Microfluidics

Zikrullah Bölükkaya, Éva Bozó, Éva Bozó, Elitsa Y. Dimova, Rolle Rahikainen — 2026-11-06 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1021/acs.nanolett.6c03702

Abstract The modular assembly of protein nanoparticles has enabled the design of highly immunogenic and cross-protective viral vaccines. However, downstream translation is hampered by labor-intensive purification methods that lack the resolution needed to efficiently sort molecular assemblies. Here, we report an affinity tag-free nanoparticle sorting platform that exploits electro-viscoelastic microfluidic (EVM) migration for the high-fidelity, continuous-flow isolation of virus-like particle (VLP) assemblies. This migration mechanism can overcome Brownian fluctuations, which are typically dominant on the nanoscale, confining the target assemblies to central streamlines for selective collection. In a single pass, the system segregates 40 nm VLPs from 6 nm excess antigen monomers, increasing the VLP purity by 20.2%. A sacrificial electrode outlet directs undesired electrohydrodynamic effects to the waste, preserving the VLP-rich product stream. Our results offer an alternative downstream framework for the rapid purification and quality control of complex bionanomaterials, establishing a scalable pathway for precision nanomedicine processing.

Prevalence and Associated Factors of Full Immunization Coverage among children aged 12–23 months in Kenya: A multi-level logistic regression analysis

Gebeyehu Lakew, Demis Getachew, Eyob Getachew, Abiyu Abadi Tareke, Amlaku Nigusie Yirsaw — 2026-11-06 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1371/journal.pone.0361187

BACKGROUND: Full immunization coverage remains suboptimal in Kenya, despite the availability of effective vaccines. Understanding the multilevel factors influencing immunization uptake is essential for strengthening child health interventions. This study examines individual and community-level determinants of full immunization coverage among children aged 12-23 months in Kenya. OBJECTIVE: To assess the individual and community-level factors associated with full immunization coverage among children aged from 12-23 months in Kenya using a multilevel analytical approach. METHODS: A cross-sectional study was conducted using nationally representative survey data from Kenya. A two-level binary logistic regression model was used to identify individual- and community-level factors associated with full immunization coverage in Kenya. Adjusted odds ratios (AOR) with 95% confidence intervals (CI) were reported. RESULTS: The prevalence of full immunization coverage among children aged from 12-23 months in Kenya was 79.4% (95% CI: 78.13-81.25). A total of 3790 weighted children aged from 12-23 months were included. Children whose mothers were exposed to media (AOR = 1.16, 95% CI: 1.01-1.34), delivered in health facilities (AOR = 2.88, 95% CI: 2.43-3.42), and attended antenatal care (AOR = 3.15, 95% CI: 2.10-4.73) had higher odds of full immunization. Children of average birth size (AOR = 1.68, 95% CI: 1.40-2.01) and those from rural areas (AOR = 1.44, 95% CI: 1.22-1.69) were also more likely to be fully immunized. Higher maternal education showed a strong positive association: primary (AOR = 1.60), secondary (AOR = 2.14), and higher education (AOR = 1.74). Older maternal age increased immunization odds: 25-34 years (AOR = 1.49), 35-49 years (AOR = 2.15). Conversely, households with more than two under-five children (AOR = 0.72) and higher parity (2-5 children AOR = 0.78; 6 + AOR = 0.67) had lower odds. Community-level low maternal education also reduced the odds (AOR = 0.79). CONCLUSION AND RECOMMENDATION: Maternal age, education, parity, media exposure, antenatal care, place of delivery, place of residence, and community education levels were associated with full immunization coverage in Kenya. Interventions should focus on improving maternal education, strengthening ANC services, promoting institutional deliveries, and enhancing outreach through media and community engagement.

Real-world safety outcomes of COVID-19 mRNA vaccine: A result from Korea post-marketing surveillance

Sang Hoon Han, Jang Hee Hong, Sook In Jung, Mo Ji-Hun, Juwon Woo — 2026-11-06 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1080/21645515.2026.2745407

The COVID-19 pandemic has caused a substantial global health burden, highlighting the need for post-authorization safety evaluation in real-world settings. BNT162b2 is a widely used mRNA COVID-19 vaccine, and, in addition to the original strain-based formulation, BA.1-, BA.4/5-, XBB.1.5-, and JN.1-adapted formulations were introduced sequentially. This study aimed to evaluate the real-world safety profile of the BNT162b2 product family using post-marketing surveillance data collected in Korea. Data collection was conducted from 50 sites. Solicited adverse events (AEs) were collected for 7 days, and unsolicited AEs for 28 days after vaccination. AEs occurring beyond Day 28 were also collected if reported during routine follow-up, without restriction on the collection period. Of 6,343 enrolled participants, 6,251 were included in the safety analysis set. Overall AEs were reported in 1,640 participants (26.2%) and adverse drug reactions (ADRs) in 1,610 participants (25.8%). Serious AEs occurred in 2 participants (<0.1%), and serious ADRs in 1 participant (<0.1%); no unexpected serious ADRs were identified. Solicited AEs were predominantly mild reactogenicity events, most commonly injection site pain, myalgia, fatigue, and headache. The overall safety profile of the BNT162b2 product family was generally manageable in this predominantly adult population, with very few serious ADRs and no unexpected serious ADRs identified. However, due to limited systematic capture of adverse events of special interest (AESIs) and underrepresentation of pediatric participants, AESI-related findings and pediatric safety conclusions should be interpreted with caution. These findings provide complementary real-world evidence regarding the safety of BNT162b2-adapted vaccines in Korea.

Harnessing Natural Killer Cells for HIV-1 Treatment and Vaccine Development: A Review

Christopher Nieva, Emma Lai, Melika Sarkandi, Adrian Francisco, Qamrul Choudhury — 2026-11-06 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.3390/v18111237

Human immunodeficiency virus type 1 (HIV-1) causes progressive immune dysfunction and, if untreated, can lead to acquired immunodeficiency syndrome (AIDS). Although combination antiretroviral therapy (cART) effectively suppresses viral replication and has substantially improved outcomes for people living with HIV (PLWH), it does not eradicate persistent viral reservoirs and requires lifelong treatment. Consequently, research continues to explore immunotherapeutic strategies that may enhance antiviral immunity and contribute to HIV-1 prevention, remission, or eradication. Natural killer (NK) cells have emerged as promising candidates in HIV-1 research because of their innate cytotoxic activity, functional diversity, adaptability, and expression of receptors involved in antiviral responses, including the NKG2 receptor family and CD16. Several NK cell-based immunotherapeutic approaches are currently under investigation, with varying mechanisms and potential applications. This review examines the current landscape of NK cell immunotherapies for HIV-1 and highlights approaches demonstrating the greatest potential relevance to HIV-1 vaccine development, including a proposed conceptual model. A PubMed literature search was conducted using terms including “NK cells,” “HIV,” “HIV vaccine,” and “NK immunotherapies.” Current evidence suggests that several NK cell-based strategies demonstrate promising antiviral activity and supports continued investigation of NK cell immunotherapies for HIV-1 treatment and prevention, particularly approaches that may inform the development of an effective HIV-1 vaccine.

Variation among dengue virus-1 lineages affects the neutralization breadth of antibody responses elicited by infection and vaccination

Sélectionné · envoyé par email

Tyshawn Ferrell, Nayan Mallubhotla, Diana Rojas-Gallardo, Daniel Espinoza, Elizabeth D. Somsen — 2026-11-06 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.21203/rs.3.rs-11323756/v1

Abstract Dengue virus (DENV) genetic diversity within serotypes may influence the breadth and durability of vaccine-induced immunity. We developed a panel of DENV-1 reporter virus particles encompassing ~ 74% of known global envelope protein diversity and evaluated neutralization using sera from tetravalent vaccine recipients, experimental DENV-1 challenge participants, and individuals with monotypic natural infection. Neutralization varied substantially among DENV-1 lineages and differed by exposure history. Several variants showed reduced neutralization following vaccination despite high overall antibody titers. Subsequent DENV-1 challenge broadened responses, which remained elevated through 180 days, whereas challenge of unvaccinated individuals produced lower potency and more lineage-specific decreases over time. Natural infection produced comparatively uniform neutralization across the panel. We used antigenic cartography to identify specific envelope amino acid positions associated with increased antigenic distance and reduced neutralization across vaccine and challenge sera. These findings demonstrate substantial within-serotype antigenic variation and support using diverse lineages to assess DENV vaccine-induced immunity.

Immunotherapeutics

Rafiq Ahmad, Muhammad Hayat, Sohrab Khan, Ghufran Uddin, Arif Ullah — 2026-11-06 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1201/9781003651116-7

The growing rate of antibiotic-resistant bacteria is a serious global health problem. Many pathogens have developed resistance to or can escape the usual antibiotics through various processes including genetic mutation, horizontal gene transfer, and active efflux, thus making them highly resistant to previously effective treatment methods and leaving patients with limited treatment options. In this chapter, we discuss the shortcomings of contemporary antibiotic treatments and the fact that new interventions are urgently needed, especially focusing on immunotherapeutic interventions as a new perspective of the fight against antimicrobial resistance. There are broad immunotherapies that may be categorized as passive and active modalities. In passive immunotherapy, immune effectors, such as monoclonal antibodies or adoptive immune cells, are directly transferred and therefore provide rapid but temporary protection. In contrast, active immunotherapy activates or primes the host immune system by using vaccines, cytokine therapies, or immune checkpoint inhibitors, thus generating long-term and pathogen-specific immunity. The main benefits of these strategies compared to conventional antibiotics are that they are more specific, have longer durations, adapt to a variety of pathogens, and in most cases have fewer adverse effects.

From Point Anomalies to Anomalous Driving Episodes: Interpreting Abnormal Driving from Vehicle Telematics

Ian Weng Chan, Andrei Badescu — 2026-11-06 — arxiv

DOI : —

Vehicle telematics provides detailed observations of driving behaviour that can be useful for automobile insurance risk assessment. Existing approaches often summarize these data through counts of harsh events or individually unusual observations, although driving movements naturally develop over time. Building on a previously proposed hidden Markov model (HMM) framework that identifies pointwise anomalies, we identify short intervals in which a sequence of observations is unusual when considered together. We then examine the original acceleration signals to measure the magnitude and temporal concentration of these episodes and identify recurring patterns of vehicle movement. Temporal concentration describes whether acceleration activity is distributed evenly across an episode or concentrated in relatively few observations. Using a controlled telematics dataset containing normal, aggressive, and drowsy driving by the same drivers, we evaluate the proposed measures through comparisons matched by driver and road type. We find that statistical anomaly measures depend on the driving population used to train the HMM. More importantly, mean temporal concentration alone distinguishes normal from abnormal driving more effectively than the original pointwise anomaly indices across different reference populations. The recurring movement patterns further reveal different characteristics of abnormal driving, with stronger longitudinal acceleration during aggressive driving and more frequent lateral corrections or excursions during drowsy driving. The framework therefore goes beyond pointwise anomaly detection, providing interpretable behavioural features that can be calculated for unlabeled trips, used for trip-level assessment, aggregated across a driver's trip history, and subsequently evaluated alongside existing telematics and rating variables for insurance risk modelling and ratemaking.

Online Causal Configuration for Networked Systems via Doubly Robust Steady-State Learning

Yuli Liu, Zhiheng Zhang — 2026-11-06 — arxiv

DOI : —

Measurement exploration reveals how interacting local controllers behave but can alter the steady-state regime a network operator seeks to optimize. Exploratory rewards may therefore fail to identify a configuration's no-exploration value: under full exploration, identical logged distributions can conceal different target values. Identification requires accounting for both action-law mismatch and shifts in the steady-state context distribution. We introduce CAUSAL-UCB, which selects configurations using optimistic bounds on target causal values. Its interference-aware clipped doubly robust evaluator produces predecision certificates separating statistical variation from calibrated identification, transport, and temporal-dependence errors. For any finite horizon and predetermined schedule of measurement-exploration rates $\{η_t\}$, we prove lower bounds of $Ω((1+\sum_tη_t)^{-1/2})$ on minimax inference error and $Ω(\sum_tη_t)$ on measurement-induced deployment loss in a stationary, potentially slow-mixing family. We bound regret under valid certificates and selected-information growth, and establish an anytime finite-sample certificate with a shared radius for estimation, selection, and reporting in a restricted known-local-interference model. In a high-mismatch benchmark with 24 paired seeds and 200 rounds, CAUSAL-UCB attains target regret of $1.732$ versus $6.326$ for certified IPS-UCB, with deployment gaps of $30.607$ and $23.217$, respectively. Independent-audit coverage tests and trace-driven semi-synthetic studies further assess certificate coverage and deployment trade-offs.

Locally Adaptive Conformal Prediction for Polynomial Chaos Expansions

Dimitrios Loukrezis, Arthur Hatstatt, Bruno Sudret, Dimitris G. Giovanis — 2026-11-06 — arxiv

DOI : —

This work develops a locally adaptive jackknife+ conformal prediction framework for polynomial chaos expansions. Therein, the non-conformity scores are normalized with an auxiliary polynomial chaos expansion trained on the log-transformed leave-one-out residuals of the base model. We show that the coverage guarantee of jackknife+ conformal prediction carries over to the adaptive intervals if the leave-one-out auxiliary models are trained on nested leave-one-out residuals. For fixed-basis polynomial chaos expansions fitted by ordinary least squares, all required quantities are derived in closed form and the guarantee-preserving adaptive intervals are obtained from a single model fit, without any model retraining. For sparse polynomial chaos expansions, where multiple model fits are necessary, we propose a heuristic simplification that limits the computational overhead of adaptivity at the cost of the formal coverage guarantee. Numerical experiments on an academic benchmark with known heteroscedastic noise and on an engineering simulation dataset show that the suggested method attains the target coverage while providing intervals that adapt to local prediction uncertainty, therefore being considerably more statistically efficient. These advantages are particularly prominent in high-uncertainty regions of the input space, where the performance of non-adaptive methods degrades substantially.

Circular interval-valued regression with application to wind direction: A cylindrical approach

Thasmika Mohan, Najmeh Nakhaei Rad, Sphiwe Skhosana — 2026-11-06 — arxiv

DOI : —

Many real-world datasets are inherently interval-valued, yet standard statistical methods are typically designed for point observations. Furthermore, intervals that are circular in nature may naturally exist. In this paper, we introduce a cylindrical regression framework for interval-valued circular data, addressing the joint challenges of the periodical nature and measurement uncertainty within the observed circular intervals. The proposed model accommodates a circular interval-valued response variable and both circular and linear covariates using a centre-range approach. To estimate the model, a maximum likelihood approach is proposed. The performance of the proposed estimation procedure is demonstrated through a simulation study. Application to two real-world datasets from a wind farm in South Africa and a weather station in Brazil further substantiates the model's practical value.

Resolving non-neutrality across scales in a hyperdiverse tropical forest

Pietro Zgaga, Pablo Villegas, Tancredi Caruso, Tommaso Gili — 2026-11-06 — arxiv

DOI : —

Different ecological processes operate at different spatial and temporal scales. Macroscopic regularities can therefore be captured by descriptions that ignore microscopic details, even when those details are mechanistically relevant to ecological processes. Here, we develop a framework inspired by statistical mechanics to track interspecific spatial correlations under spatial coarse-graining. To do so we combine random-matrix filtering with a spatially explicit neutral model. Applying this approach to eight censuses of the Barro Colorado Island tropical forest, we observe a continuous crossover from an interacting to an effectively neutral organization. Below a few tens of metres (20--35~m), the empirical correlation spectrum differs from neutral expectations, revealing scale-dependent species-specific structure. Part of this structure can be attributed to environmental heterogeneity. However, residual spatial associations provide candidate signatures of biotic interactions or other unmeasured ecological, non-neutral processes. With increasing spatial coarse-graining, empirical and neutral correlation spectra progressively converge, and above the crossover scale the neutral model provides an accurate description of the measured collective organization. Neutrality thus emerges as an effective description of the collective organization of the forest above a characteristic scale, without implying that species are equivalent at the individual level. Our framework provides an operational way to locate that scale.

Debias Online Expectile Regression for High-Dimensional Stream Data and Statistic Inference

Wei Cao, Shanshan Wang — 2026-11-06 — arxiv

DOI : —

Expectile regression is a powerful tool for capturing heterogeneous structures in data and has attracted increasing attention in large-scale data analysis due to its computational efficiency. However, statistical inference for high-dimensional online expectile regression remains underexplored. In this paper, we propose a debiased online expectile regression method for high-dimensional streaming data. The proposed method incorporates Lasso regularization to perform variable selection and alleviate estimation instability caused by high dimensionality. It further adopts an online updating strategy in which recursively updated cumulative statistics are used to approximate the global gradient, and a proximal gradient descent algorithm is employed to update model parameters. To enable valid inference when only the current batch and historical summary statistics are available, we also develop an online precision matrix estimation procedure. We establish the consistency and asymptotic normality of the proposed debiased estimator. Extensive numerical studies and real-data applications show that the proposed method achieves performance comparable to the oracle and offline estimators while maintaining low storage cost.

New optimisations for exact, scalable 2D Gaussian process inference, with applications to astronomical time-series

Mark Fortune, Neale P. Gibson, Benjamin Pope — 2026-11-06 — arxiv

DOI : —

Astronomical datasets often consist of a grid of datapoints in two or more dimensions, such as images, spectroscopic time-series, and imaging time-series. Gaussian processes (GPs) are a powerful tool for dealing with correlated noise in these data, arising from both astrophysical and instrumental systematics. Despite this, popular GP optimisations such as celerite are largely restricted to 1D datasets. Extensions to 2D data typically come with limited flexibility or poor scaling. As a motivating example, this limitation is significant for transiting exoplanet spectroscopy with JWST, as current analyses typically assume each spectroscopic light curve is statistically independent, despite many potential sources of wavelength-correlation, including detector readout noise and stellar granulation. This introduces a potential bias in atmospheric retrievals which is typically ignored. To solve this problem, we present new, exact GP optimisations implemented in the JAX-based package luas, which extend 1D GP optimisations to multidimensional data using Kronecker product algebra. These GP optimisations are highly flexible, permit efficient analysis of 2D datasets containing millions of datapoints, and in many cases have a cheaper cost than transit light curve modelling. These optimisations are key to pushing the limits of exoplanet spectroscopy to smaller targets around bright host stars, as we increasingly hit instrumental and astrophysical noise floors. We suggest further applications to the analysis of ground-based high resolution spectroscopic time-series, extreme precision radial velocities, and high-contrast imaging, drawing on the connection that certain 2D GP models represent a probabilistic generalisation of PCA. Applications may even extend to machine learning and geostatistics, as we present novel optimisations for the Linear Mixing Model and the Linear Model of Coregionalisation.

Group Invariance of Statistical Models and Associated Statistical Manifolds

Zhuohao Cheng — 2026-11-06 — arxiv

DOI : —

We study the group invariance of a parameterized statistical model that is closed under push-forward sample transformations given by a group action. We prove that the group action on the sample space induces an isometric action on the associated statistical manifold. Furthermore, it preserves the Fisher-Rao metric as well as the Amari-Chentsov tensor. Moreover, we establish that this isometric action is compatible with equivariant and sufficient statistics in the spirit of the Hall-Wijsman-Ghosh generalized Stein's theorem. This compatibility leads to a novel reduction of the original group-invariant model, where its Fisher information loss is explicitly quantified via conditional expectation. As an application, we apply this framework to a generalized cardioid distribution in circular data analysis, illustrating how the reduced model facilitates estimation and hypothesis testing for relative parameters.

Ordered Policy Optimization

Dongkyu Derek Cho, Pan Xu — 2026-11-06 — arxiv

DOI : —

Proximal Policy Optimization (PPO) stabilizes policy updates by clipping the importance ratio between the new policy and the data-collecting policy. The standard rule uses one fixed clipping range for all samples. While this choice offers simplicity and effectiveness, it fails to distinguish statistically harmful extreme ratio values from large, yet tolerable changes. We study how to adapt the clipping radius to the reliability of the induced importance ratio distribution. Our case study demonstrates that reliable updates require controlling these extreme ratios, rather than merely verifying that individual ratios fall within a fixed interval. Motivated by this observation, we propose Ordered Policy Optimization (\textsc{OPO}). In each minibatch, \textsc{OPO} sorts the importance ratios, selects the extreme ratios that potentially have the largest effect on the update, and assigns them adaptive sample-wise radii instead of using the fixed clipping radius. These radii are derived from a prescribed tail profile, which specifies desired target values for the selected extreme ratios. The resulting objective remains first-order and requires only minibatch sorting and a modified policy loss. Experiments conducted on MuJoCo and Atari benchmarks demonstrate that this simple modification achieves strong performance relative to established baselines.

PH-Symb : Sparse symbolic identification of nonlinear port-Hamiltoniansystems using Kolmogorov-Arnold networks

Sélectionné · envoyé par email

Achraf El Messaoudi, Karim Cherifi, Yann Le Gorrec, Yongxin Wu — 2026-11-06 — arxiv

DOI : —

The paper introduces PH-Symb, a novel symbolic structure-preserving identification method, that combines Kolmogorov Arnold networks and sparse regression to build explicit port-Hamiltonian (pH) models from data. Unlike other Machine Learning or neural pH approaches, PH-Symb recovers physically interpretable and explicit-symbolic components and enforces pH structure by construction. It also introduces a new normalization method that preserves the pH structure. It allows handling both identification from input-state-output (ISO) data and input-output (IO) data, and applications show it delivers interpretable models and lower errors than neural baselines on synthetic and real-world systems.

Multiomics Integration Reveals a Skeletal Muscle Metabolic Myopathy in Cardiometabolic HFpEF

Sélectionné · envoyé par email

Timothy Daniel Allerton, Daniel A. Arabie, Kade Malone, Abhishek Pandit, Mahmoud H. Elbatreek — 2026-11-06 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1161/circheartfailure.125.014126

Background: Skeletal muscle dysfunction is a major peripheral determinant of exercise intolerance and physical disability in heart failure with preserved ejection fraction (HFpEF). Metabolic and mitochondrial dysfunction are considered to be key components of skeletal muscle dysfunction, but comprehensive profiling of metabolic pathways has not been conducted. Elucidation of dysregulated metabolic pathways is essential to determine viable targets for the treatment of exercise intolerance in HFpEF. Methods: was used to determine differential expression in all omics layers (absolute fold-change>1.5, FDR0.05, unless otherwise indicated). Additional targeted plasma and skeletal muscle (soleus and EDL) metabolomics and lipidomics were performed on HFpEF and control rats. Results: Pathway level analysis for RNA seq and proteomics revealed significant downregulation of oxidative phosphorylation (NES -2.1, p<0.005), electron transport chain (NES -2.0, p<0.005), and TCA cycle (-1.8, p<0.05). The most upregulated pathways were PPAR signaling (NES 2.2, p<0.0001), tryptophan metabolism (NES 1.8, P<0.005), and amino acid oxidation (NES 1.8, p<0.005) pathways. Metabolomics revealed an accumulation of TCA cycle intermediate, isocitrate, and phosphate reduction. Branched-chain amino acids were significantly increased, whereas amino acids related to tryptophan metabolism were reduced and shifted towards increased serotonin accumulation. Phospholipid species were differentially regulated with increased palmitoylated phosphatidylcholines but reduced arachidonoyl-PC species. Phosphatidylethanolamines (PE) species (16:0/16:1-18:0/18:2) were increased. Conclusion: Our multiomics analysis of skeletal muscle in HFpEF revealed severe mitochondrial dysfunction that was characterized by reduced complex I and II activity. Mitochondrial and peroxisomal lipid overload results in a shift in membrane phospholipid accumulation and composition. Reduced BCAA oxidation and dysregulation of tryptophan metabolism are key features of amino acid metabolism that reduce anaplerosis and promote the accumulation of toxic metabolites. Comparative analysis of other skeletal muscle disorders suggests that an acquired metabolic myopathy exists in cardiometabolic HFpEF.

Blood-brain barrier disrupting stimuli induce production of extracellular vesicles with distinct protein cargoes and functionality

Alec Xiang, Zara Adeel, Kazuya Abe, Andrew Shih, Betty Diamond — 2026-11-06 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1186/s12987-026-00903-5

Abstract The blood-brain barrier (BBB) protects the brain from substances in the circulation; yet, this barrier can be breached. Systemic lupus erythematosus is a disease in which over 50% of patients experience manifestations of neuropsychiatric lupus (NPSLE), with many displaying BBB dysfunction. High levels of TNFα, IL-1β, C5a, and epinephrine, which have all been shown to cause BBB permeability, may be present in the serum of SLE patients. Brain microvascular endothelial cells (BMVECs) are a major structural element of the BBB. We asked whether BMVECs stimulated with barrier-disrupting factors release extracellular vesicles (EVs) which might participate in autocrine signaling or might have utility as diagnostic biomarkers of BBB permeability. We analyzed proteins in EVs secreted by unstimulated and stimulated BMVECs by mass spectrometry and used secreted EVs for further BMVEC stimulation. We subjected BMVECs to bulk RNA-seq to identify response signatures to the initial stimulus and to secondary stimulation with EVs. We show that there are agent-specific EV-associated protein profiles. EVs from BMVECs subjected to BBB-breaching stimuli alter the BMVEC transcriptional program, representing a potential feed-forward mechanism. Finally, we suggest that the proteins associated with EVs might be markers of BBB disruption.

Ehrlichia infection progression drives tissue-independent transcriptional heterogeneity in tick cells

Abdulsalam Adegoke, Joseph A. Aspinwall, Colton McNinch, Margaret C.W. Ho, Adam X. Miranda — 2026-11-06 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.3389/fcimb.2026.1961724

ABSTRACT Tick-borne diseases represent a significant threat to human and animal health worldwide. In the United States, the blacklegged tick, Ixodes scapularis ( I. scapularis) , serves as a competent vector for several bacterial pathogens, including Ehrlichia muris eauclairensis (EME). The I. scapularis embryonic cell line (ISE6) is a valuable tool for propagating tick-borne pathogens and studying tick-pathogen interactions. In this study, we examined the cellular complexity of ISE6 cells and their response to EME infection. Single-cell RNA sequencing revealed 15 distinct cell clusters present. Although ISE6 cells are heterogeneous, they do not display transcriptional similarity to any known tick tissues. Notably, this lack of similarity did not influence their susceptibility to EME infection. Our results demonstrated that EME infection induces time-dependent transcriptional changes in ISE6 cells: early infection is characterized by upregulation of genes associated with stress adaptation, mitochondrial function, and metabolic pathways, whereas late infection leads to broad downregulation of genes involved in the cell cycle, DNA replication, and cytoskeletal organization. These findings enhance our understanding of ehrlichial interactions with ISE6 cells and reinforce the utility of this cell line as a resource for isolating and propagating arthropod endosymbionts and tick-borne pathogens. IMPORTANCE This study provides a single-cell resolution framework for interpreting tick cell line biology during infection with a medically relevant ehrlichial pathogen. Using scRNA-seq, we show that the I. scapularis embryonic-derived ISE6 cell line comprises multiple transcriptionally distinct cell states, yet these states do not map cleanly onto canonical tick tissue signatures, even when compared against a curated reference tissue atlas. Despite this heterogeneity, EME broadly infects ISE6 cell population, indicating that susceptibility is not restricted to a specific cell type. We further define a time-dependent arthropod vector response in which early infection is marked by activation of stress and metabolic adaptation response, followed by late-stage inhibition of key signaling, transcriptional, and proliferative pathways as bacterial burden increases. Together, these findings strengthen the biological interpretation of ISE6 as an in vitro model for tick–pathogen interactions and provide a resource for future mechanistic studies of ehrlichial persistence, replication, and vector competence.

Identification of Novel Human-Specific Long Non-Coding RNAs in Spinal Cord Astrocytoma Through RNA-Seq Analysis

Vaibhavi Jain, Archana N. Panche, Abhishek Nagargoje — 2026-11-06 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.21203/rs.3.rs-11631304/v1

Abstract Human spinal cord aging is associated with molecular changes that may affect neuronal function and tissue homeostasis, but the role of long non-coding RNAs (lncRNAs) in this process remains poorly understood. In this study, RNA-sequencing data from young and old human spinal cord samples were analyzed using an integrated bioinformatics pipeline, including transcript assembly, novel lncRNA identification, differential expression analysis, co-expression analysis, functional enrichment, evolutionary conservation, and SNP analysis. A total of 1,669 novel lncRNAs were identified, among which three transcripts (MSTRG.24062.6, MSTRG.35781.3, and MSTRG.57931.6) were significantly differentially expressed between the young and old age groups. Functional enrichment of co-expressed genes highlighted processes related to protein targeting to membrane, synaptic signaling, neuron projection organization, calcium ion transport, and membrane potential regulation. KEGG pathway analysis identified adrenergic signaling in cardiomyocytes as the most significantly enriched pathway. Conservation analysis revealed that all identified novel lncRNAs were human-specific and unmapped to the mouse genome. Genetic variant analysis identified 11,243 variants across 496 novel lncRNAs, with SNPs accounting for more than 95%. Transitions were more frequent than transversions, and variant distribution varied across chromosomes and lncRNAs. A weak positive correlation between SNP count and lncRNA expression suggested a limited association between genetic variation and expression levels. Overall, this study provides a comprehensive characterization of novel lncRNAs associated with human spinal cord aging and establishes a foundation for future studies investigations into their potential regulatory roles in age related molecular changes in spinal cord aging.

PmiREN3.0: a multi-omics integrated and AI-assisted encyclopedia for plant microRNA research

Chuan Zhang, Jiawen Zhao, Xingchen Yu, Qi Wang, Anyu Zhao — 2026-11-06 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1093/nar/gkag1133

MicroRNAs (miRNAs) are key post-transcriptional regulators in gene regulatory networks (GRNs). Comprehensive research on miRNAs increasingly requires the integration of large and heterogeneous datasets, together with more efficient approaches to knowledge exploration. Here, we present PmiREN3.0, a major update of the Plant MicroRNA Encyclopedia. Compared with PmiREN2.0, species/subspecies coverage increased from 179 to 340, adding 22 orders, 51 families, and 108 genera. Using the same standardized workflow and annotation criteria as previous releases, high-confidence miRNAs were identified from 5319 small RNA sequencing (sRNA-seq) datasets, with miRNA loci increasing from 39 751 to 87 667 and miRNA families from 7838 to 18 389. Resources and tools from previous releases were extensively updated, while six new modules were added for primary transcripts, DNA methylation, RNA modifications, stress-responsive expression, metabolic pathways, and core GRNs. PmiREN3.0 also introduces miRai+, a knowledge service powered by artificial intelligence (AI) that combines a language model fine-tuned on miRNA knowledge with retrieval augmented generation. Evaluation yielded scores of 0.910 for answer relevance, 0.965 for faithfulness, and 0.906 for factual correctness. Together, PmiREN3.0 provides an expanded plant miRNA dataset and an integrated platform combining multi-omics data and AI-assisted knowledge services for plant miRNA research. PmiREN3.0 is available at https://pmiren.com/.

Comparative transcriptome analysis reveals candidate genes and defence pathways associated with resistance to potato cyst nematodes in potato (Solanum tuberosum)

Salej Sood, Bhawna Dipta, Aarti Bairwa, Vinay Bhardwaj, Barkha Sharma — 2026-11-06 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1071/fp26022

Globodera rostochiensis and Globodera pallida are two nematode species responsible for are potato cyst nematode (PCN) that significantly reduce potato (Solanum tuberosum) productivity worldwide. To elucidate the molecular basis of PCN resistance, we compared transcriptomic responses of the highly resistant potato breeding clone SM/11-120 with the susceptible cultivar Kufri Jyoti following simultaneous infection with both PCN species. Nematode penetration assays showed that SM/11-120 completely restricted nematode invasion up to 7 days post-inoculation (dpi), whereas Kufri Jyoti was susceptible to nematode penetration within 24 h post-inoculation (hpi) and supported juvenile development at 72 hpi and 7 dpi. RNA-sequencing (RNA-Seq) generated 18-27 million high-quality paired-end reads per library, of which 51.6-68.3% mapped uniquely to the potato reference genome. Comparative transcriptome analysis identified 724, 795, 694, and 899 differentially expressed genes (DEGs) between SM/11-120 and Kufri Jyoti at 0 hpi, 24 hpi, 72 hpi, and 7 dpi, respectively. The resistant genotype exhibited enhanced expression of genes involved in immune signalling (NRC1, NBS-LRR), transcriptional regulation (WRKY, MYB), calcium signalling, pathogenesis-related proteins (PR1), hormone and defence metabolism (ACO1, geraniol 10-hydroxylase), and cell wall reinforcement (HRGP, XTH3, pectinesterase, β-expansin). Gene Ontology analysis revealed stronger enrichment of stress response, biological regulation, nitrogen compound metabolism, and response to biotic stimulus in SM/11-120 than in Kufri Jyoti. Expression patterns of selected defence-related genes were validated by quantitative reverse transcription (qRT)-PCR, confirming their rapid and sustained induction in the resistant genotype.

Annexin A1-Mediated Therapeutic Effect of Extracellular Vesicles Derived from Thrombin- Preconditioned Mesenchymal Stromal Cells in Experimental Necrotizing Enterocolitis

Choleun Kwon, Sein Hwang, Young Eun Kim, Misun Yang, So Yoon Ahn — 2026-11-06 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.21203/rs.3.rs-10911604/v1

Abstract Background Necrotizing enterocolitis (NEC) is a devastating inflammatory disease in premature infants lacking specific treatment. Extracellular vesicles from thrombin-preconditioned mesenchymal stromal cells (thMSC-EVs) show enhanced therapeutic efficacy over naïve MSC-EVs. This study aimed to identify the key enriched therapeutic factor and its mechanism in experimental NEC. Methods Comparative proteomic profiling (LC-MS/MS) of thMSC-EVs was performed to identify candidate proteins. NEC was induced in neonatal mice (P4) using formula feeding, LPS, and stress. thMSC-EVs were administered intraperitoneally. Bulk RNA-sequencing and multi-omics ligand-receptor analyses of the mouse ileum integrated proteomic and transcriptomic data. The candidate, Annexin A1, was validated using in vitro LPS-induced inflammation and multiple-hit (HIT) models, assessing viability, barrier integrity, and NF-κB and Wnt/β-catenin signaling following treatment with thMSC-EVs, the Annexin A1 mimetic peptide (Ac2-26), and ANXA1-knockdown MSC-derived EVs (siANXA1-MSC-EVs). Results Proteomic analysis showed thMSC-EVs were significantly enriched with anti-inflammatory proteins, including a 3.8-fold increase in ANXA1. In the NEC model, thMSC-EVs attenuated intestinal necrosis and apoptosis, suppressed the mucosal antimicrobial response, and promoted tissue repair. Multi-omics ligand-receptor analysis identified the Annexin A1–formyl peptide receptor 1 interaction as the most prominent therapeutic axis. In vitro, Ac2-26 replicated the effects of thMSC-EVs, restoring cell viability and ZO-1 expression and suppressing NF-κB while restoring Wnt/β-catenin signaling. Conversely, siANXA1-MSC-EVs showed a significantly, though partially, diminished therapeutic effect. Conclusion Thrombin preconditioning amplifies the ANXA1 content within MSC-EVs. This enriched ANXA1 exerts a dual therapeutic effect in NEC by suppressing TLR4/NF-kB-mediated inflammation and promoting Wnt/β-catenin-mediated epithelial regeneration via the FPR signaling. Our findings establish ANXA1 as a major, though not exclusive, effector of thMSC-EVs and as a novel therapeutic target for NEC, supporting thMSC-EVs as a potent cell-free therapeutic agent.

Polar tip growth is crucial for pollen tube elongation during fertilization and root hair elongation for nutrient and water uptake in plants. Previous work identified a Cyclin-Dependent Kinase-like (CDK-like) gene, BUZZ, in Brachypodium distachyon. My research focuses on characterizing the putative BUZZ ortholog rth2 in Zea mays.
