Fast Functional Clustering via Closed-Form Covariance Updates

Yu Wang, Jiaze Guo, Yao Chen, Tianxiang Hao, Rongling Wu — 2026-11-05 — arxiv

DOI : —

Functional clustering identifies coordinated molecular profiles, but repeated numerical covariance optimization can make mixture-model fitting computationally demanding. We develop a fast functional clustering procedure for joint analysis across conditions, combining linear mean profiles on a standardized log niche coordinate with first-order structured antedependence (SAD(1)) covariance. Weighted residual statistics yield closed-form conditional covariance updates under four parameter-sharing specifications, while an innovation representation simplifies likelihood evaluation. In same-R comparisons with numerical optimization of identical conditional objectives, the closed-form solver was 91.5--201.4 times as fast, with objective agreement in all 60 targets. Under matched complete mixture fits, it achieved a 1.87--2.37-fold speedup with identical cluster assignments. Recovery simulations showed higher module classification accuracy and lower mean-curve error than $K$-means and a Gaussian mixture with shared full covariance when the mean structure was correctly specified. SAD(1) provided an additional recovery gain under strong residual correlation, whereas curved-mean controls identified the importance of an appropriate mean family. Applications to single-condition gut microbiota, paired leaf--root microbiota and three-visit human gut multi-omics illustrate the framework across different numbers of conditions. Closed-form covariance estimation makes joint functional clustering substantially faster while retaining the fitted model's likelihood and assignments.

Continuous-time optimal execution and utility-based statistical arbitrage in a Markovian environment

Masaaki Fukasawa, Masamitsu Ohnishi, Makoto Shimoshimizu — 2026-11-05 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1080/14697688.2026.2746476

This paper examines a continuous-time execution problem for a risk-averse large trader in the presence of temporary, permanent, and transient market impacts as well as an exogenous, stochastic factor that affects the financial asset price. The stochastic factor, which we refer to as the Markovian environment, is described by a generalized Ornstein–Uhlenbeck (OU) process. The problem is formulated as a continuous-time stochastic control problem over a finite horizon. By analyzing the associated Hamilton–Jacobi–Bellman (HJB) equation, we derive an optimal execution strategy characterized by an affine function of the state variables with time-dependent coefficients. The time-dependent coefficients are determined by a solution to a system of numerically tractable ordinary differential equations (ODEs) with terminal conditions, consisting of (i) a matrix Riccati differential equation (MRDE), (ii) a vector-valued linear ODE, and (iii) a one-dimensional ODE. Under a set of assumptions, we show a sufficient condition under which the MRDE admits a unique solution and establish a verification theorem. Simulation-based numerical experiments illustrate that the Markovian environment significantly influences the optimal execution strategy. Specifically, we numerically observe the possibility of a utility-based statistical arbitrage via a round-trip trading.

Robust Outside-Range Optimization by Decoupling Search-Region Expansion and Search-Distribution Update in AREX/JGG

Yuya Kanda, Yukako Tohsato — 2026-11-05 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.3390/app162110953

In real-coded genetic algorithms (RCGAs), search performance often deteriorates when the true global optimum lies outside the initial search range because crossover-based sampling remains concentrated around the initial population. We define this problem as outside-range optimization and introduce the outside-range ratio, representing the proportion of dimensions whose true optimum lies outside the initial search range. We extend Adaptive Real-coded Ensemble Crossover/Just Generation Gap (AREX/JGG) by explicitly decoupling search-region expansion and search-distribution update. Specifically, enhanced Chaotic Search (e-CS) expands the search region through extrapolation, AREX updates the search distribution, and Combinational Mutation (CM) generates candidates with heavy-tailed perturbations. Experiments on 10 benchmark functions with 20–80 dimensions showed lower expected running time (ERT) than AREX/JGG under many outside-range conditions examined. For 40-dimensional Sphere, Cigar, and k-tablet, ERT was lower than HIRCGA across all tested outside-range ratios and distances. Furthermore, for parameter estimation of an ordinary differential equation (ODE) model of Escherichia coli glycolysis, the proposed method achieved a higher success rate than AREX/JGG in three of four initial-range settings. These results suggest that the proposed method can improve search efficiency under many tested outside-range conditions and enhance the robustness of search success to initial-range settings.

From Multiscale Emergence to Testable Intervention

Sulei Hu, Xingao Gong, Duyu Chen, Jingguo Li, Qiyu Kang — 2026-11-05 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1021/prechem.6c00104

Abstract Precision chemistry requires more than accurate prediction: it requires a traceable chain from measurement to representation, mechanism, design, and reproducible intervention. We argue that, within a declared operating envelope, a small set of physically meaningful coordinates and explicit relations can reduce multiscale prediction and design to a closed model that can be evaluated within specified error and computational budgets. We formulate five requirements for such coordinates─predictive sufficiency, dynamical closure, interventional stability, identifiability, and reachability─and organize them in a representation–closure–search–intervention (RCSI) cycle. Its central object is an input–output description mediated by physical variables whose relations can be examined and revised. Analytic, numerical, learned, and hybrid solvers are selected according to accuracy, cost, and latency. Uncertainty and provenance are retained at every stage, and models are evaluated by extrapolation, mechanism discrimination, and intervention success. Examples from catalytic interfaces, electronic and atomistic models, polymer field theory, microscopy, active matter, biomolecular design, and quantum-control planning illustrate both the opportunity and the limits. We conclude with prospective tests that can falsify each stage and a reporting standard for closed-loop scientific AI.

Occupation Measures in the ODE Method: Wasserstein Convergence and Rates

Dimitrios Katselis — 2026-11-04 — arxiv

DOI : —

We study occupation measures associated with stochastic approximation recursions within the ordinary differential equation (ODE) method. Under Lyapunov conditions ensuring that the limiting ODE has a globally exponentially stable equilibrium, we establish that the continuous-time occupation measures associated with the piecewise linear interpolation of the iterates converge almost surely in the $2$-Wasserstein topology to the Dirac mass at the equilibrium, the unique invariant probability measure of the limiting semiflow. The choice of the $2$-Wasserstein distance is naturally aligned with our continuous- and discrete-time Lyapunov drift analysis. We also consider two discrete-time sequences of occupation measures, induced by the algorithmic time scale and the iteration count, respectively, and we establish their almost sure convergence to the same invariant measure in the $2$-Wasserstein distance. We then derive corresponding convergence rates in expectation, providing a nonasymptotic complement to the almost sure results. These expectation bounds also imply some tail probability estimates. Finally, we verify that our results apply to a canonical class of linear recursions.

Causal Inference from Self-controlled Case Series

Gellért Perényi, Marco Piccininni, Mats J. Stensrud — 2026-11-02 — arxiv

DOI : —

The self-controlled case series (SCCS) design is widely used in studies of rare events, for example of vaccine side effects. Conceptually, the design has two main features. First, it requires only data from individuals who have experienced the event of interest. Therefore, the design is especially useful when the outcome is rare and the available data come from surveillance or clinical records, say, of hospitalized patients. Second, because comparisons are made within single individuals, time-invariant confounding between treatment and outcome is eliminated. We formulate a set of sufficient identification assumptions for estimating the causal effect of vaccination on an adverse outcome under the SCCS design, drawing on ideas from the causal inference literature. Unlike the existing SCCS literature, we do not consider a parametric model as the starting point. This allows us to establish when and why the SCCS design allows identification of causal effects, using data from cases only. However, because the sufficient identifiability assumptions are implausible in some applications, we also develop statistical tests for the existence of a treatment effect that remain valid when only a subset of the identification assumptions holds.

Association between prenatal cannabis and tobacco co‐exposure and cognition in childhood: Insights from the US adolescent brain cognitive development study

Mathilde Argote, Anaïs Rubsamen, Rachel Allison Rabin — 2026-11-02 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1111/add.70692

BACKGROUND AND AIMS: While evidence on the individual effects of prenatal cannabis and tobacco exposure on offspring cognition is accumulating, their combined impact remains understudied. Therefore, we examined the interaction between prenatal cannabis and tobacco exposure on fluid (e.g. reasoning abilities) and crystallized (e.g. acquired knowledge) cognition in middle childhood. We also compared cognition in children with prenatal co-exposure to those with cannabis-only exposure, tobacco-only exposure and no exposure. DESIGN: Cross-sectional exploratory analysis of baseline data from an ongoing longitudinal cohort study. SETTING: Data collected between 2016 and 2018 from the United States-based Adolescent Brain Cognitive Development study were extracted. PARTICIPANTS: Children (n = 8706), aged 9-11 years old, were parsed into four prenatal exposure groups: co-exposure (n = 236); cannabis only (n = 195); tobacco only (n = 803); and no exposure (n = 7472). MEASUREMENTS: Prenatal co-exposure was measured as a binary variable (yes/no) based on maternal/caregiver retrospective report. Fluid and crystallized scores were derived from the National Institutes of Health (NIH) Toolbox cognitive battery; scores ranged from 44 to 131 and 51115 for fluid and crystallized cognition, respectively. We employed linear mixed-effects models to examine interactions between cannabis and tobacco exposure and group differences in fluid and crystallized scores, adjusting for covariates selected a priori using a Directed Acyclic Graph. RESULTS: For fluid cognition, a statistically significant interaction emerged between prenatal cannabis and tobacco exposure [beta coefficient (β) = -2.08, 95% confidence interval (CI) = -4.13 to -0.03]. Cannabis only (β = 2.13, 95% CI = 0.21-4.05) and unexposed children (β = 1.39, 95% CI = 0.03-2.76) had statistically significantly higher fluid scores than co-exposed children, while the difference with tobacco-only exposed children was not statistically significant (β = 1.34, 95% CI = -0.14 to 2.82). No statistically significant interaction was observed between prenatal co-exposure and crystallized cognition (β = -0.62, 95% CI = -1.87 to 0.64), and crystallized scores did not statistically significantly differ between the co-exposed group and the cannabis-only (β = 0.66, 95% CI = -0.51 to 1.84), tobacco-only (β = 0.19, 95% CI = -0.71 to 1.1) or unexposed groups (β = 0.24, 95% CI = -0.6 to 1.08). SUMMARY: Prenatal co-exposure to cannabis and tobacco appears to be associated with poorer fluid cognition among children, but not poorer crystallized cognition, compared with cannabis-only and unexposed groups. These findings should be interpreted with caution given the potential for residual confounding by shared genetic liability, substance exposure misclassification and selection bias. The cross-sectional design of this study precludes causal inference.

A resident-traveller model for global aspects of vaccination

Julien Arino, P. van den Driessche — 2026-11-01 — arxiv

DOI : —

A model for the spread of a vaccine-preventable infectious disease is set in a resident-traveller metapopulation context to investigate global aspects of vaccination. Of particular interest here is the effect of varying vaccine coverage in some patches on the spread of disease in the whole system. Analytically and numerically, the resident-traveller framework is compared with a classic metapopulation model where individuals become residents of the patch they move to. Evolutionary characterization of whole-genome doubling across human cancers

Yangyang Zhang, Haiming Li, Xiaoyu Zhao, Zilong Wang, Yongqi Chen — 2026-11-01 — arxiv

DOI : —

Whole-genome doubling (WGD) is common across human cancers and is associated with tumour progression and adverse clinical outcomes. However, how genome-doubled tumours evolve across diverse cancer types remains poorly understood. Here we characterize WGD-associated tumour evolution by integrating genomic analyses of 41,332 tumours from 12 cancer types with evolutionary timing and multi-omic data. Several canonical driver mutations generally preceded WGD when both occurred, as did the onset of homologous recombination deficiency-associated mutagenesis. Notably, post-WGD chromosome-arm losses showed substantially weaker cancer-type dependence than pre-WGD losses, with 61-75% less variance explained by cancer type. Beyond chromosomal evolution, WGD+ tumours showed reduced interferon-response and HLA antigen-presentation programmes linked to epigenetic remodelling, without disproportionate enrichment of genetic immune-escape alterations. Our findings highlight WGD as a recurrent evolutionary transition associated with altered chromosomal evolution and epigenetically linked immune adaptation across cancers.

Plant‐ and Microbial‐Derived Soil Organic Carbon: Global Patterns and Divergent Climatic Associations

Jiayuan Wang, Xuefeng Xie, Xingwen LIN, Lijie Pu, Lingyue Zhu — 2026-11-01 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1111/gcb.71174

Soil organic carbon (SOC) pools reflect the net balance of plant- and microbial-derived inputs, transformation, and loss, yet a global-scale assessment of distribution patterns, relative contributions, and hierarchical environmental correlates of microbial necromass carbon (MNC) and plant-derived carbon (PdC) remains incomplete. Based on 1167 site-level observations (1235 pool-level records) across four ecosystem types, we applied variance partitioning, random forest, linear mixed-effects models, and structural equation modeling to quantify global patterns and associative pathways of the two carbon pools. Unadjusted pooled comparisons showed higher MNC concentration and SOC contribution than PdC. However, covariate-adjusted linear mixed-effects models revealed significantly higher PdC. Paired observations showed the same directional trend, although statistical significance was not reached due to limited paired sample size (concentration: p = 0.104; contribution: p = 0.140). The apparent MNC dominance constitutes a Simpson's paradox driven by uneven ecosystem sampling, rather than a consistent within-ecosystem pattern. Instead, the relative contribution of MNC and PdC was ecosystem-dependent: MNC was marginally higher in forests, whereas PdC dominated in croplands and wetlands, with no significant difference in grasslands in paired observations. Edaphic variables were the strongest predictors (independent explanatory power: 61.1% for MNC, 47.4% for PdC), with total nitrogen, C:N ratio, pH, and mean annual temperature as key correlates. Our results reveal a hierarchical associative cascade in which geographic factors covary with climate, which in turn correlates with edaphic properties as the strongest direct predictors. Climate shows a weak but significant negative direct association with MNC only. This synthesis provides a hierarchical correlative framework and biome-specific baseline parameters to support explicit representation of plant- and microbial-mediated SOC pathways in Earth system models.
